# Completed Milestone: Independent Component Lifting Through Contracts

The small hand-defined Metapad network milestone is complete under its explicit
trusted runtime contracts. The [requirement-by-requirement audit](baselines/2026-09-14-independent-component-milestone.md)
records the four real authoring components, original/C proofs, checked memory and
control transport, actual public edit/repair/refinement workflows, meaningful
neighboring implementation reuse, and separately validated runtime premises.
Both consumers reuse unchanged proof models when the resource implementation
changes; withdrawing EDI preservation invalidates only the UI consumer.

Current readers validate all public baseline/error/repair/neighbor and refinement
artifacts without process or model/header execution. Their exact Nix/compiler
inputs and source bindings are audited. All 659 production Python modules match
the public experiment snapshot. Fifteen native tests pass, including the current
actual maximum-width allocation-failure test; 27 retained local tests have current
inputs. Four repository gates pass, with the same two pre-existing boundary-test
failures and seven unchanged size offenders recorded separately.

This completes the conditional independent-work demonstration, not general
boundary-driven composition or strong qualification. Runtime contracts remain
trusted and locally proved application behavior remains conditional; fixture-owned
image/stack translation, arbitrary callbacks/nonreturn and full caller reachability
are not universally qualified. General split/merge, richer contracts, representation
changes, unrelated-target reuse and the Hello/jq/DX-Ball/native-link/export/portability
path continue in the broader plan. No automated boundary recommendation or native
activation is authorized by this result. Earlier checkpoint text below is historical.

Resource/notice integration (2026-09-14, v120): the existing consumer runtime
fixture now reaches ordinary-C resource retrieval and actual MessageBoxA through
both original caller slices and their ordinary-C consumers. Fifteen cases run on
both sides. The normal and loop-edit resource bodies are byte-identical to the
public proof inputs; their generated interface headers also match. Nine native
allocation/image-reference bodies match the production renderer exactly.

The retained original PE is mapped using SEC_IMAGE. LoadStringA reads its real
resource 31, and the dialog check observes the current text, caption, flags, IDOK
return and ordered resource/message/focus effects. The native namespace uses the
actual mapped base and the four original section origins. The fixture translates
between retained operation addresses and the actual mapping; raw memory and stack
admission remain fixture-owned. It checks resource coordinates, current bytes and
native address transport, then rejects the saved image reference after unmapping
and removal from admission. This does not qualify arbitrary relocation, same-base
reload generations, loader initialization or general callback/nonreturn behavior.

The first source-binding audit rejected use of the older generic resource fixture
as evidence for the public implementation. Runtime validation now uses the exact
normal and loop-edit sources instead. The two consumer proofs remain importable
with process/model/header generation forbidden (6.963s and 7.400s); the 27 retained
local tests still have all 25 declared inputs current. No application proof query,
pilot or native application link was rebuilt for this runtime extension.

Ten expanded runtime tests pass in Nix, including the normal and loop-edit
implementations plus eight negative controls. Together with four unchanged earlier
adapter tests, 14 tests have all 233 declared input paths current. The baseline
prepares in 0.0374s, compiles twelve translation units in 0.630s, links in 0.0401s
and runs 30 original/C executions in 7.783s, including Wine/display startup. The
neighbor variant runs the same 30 executions in 6.581s. The boundary shard retains
exactly its two prior failures and seven unchanged size offenders. Detailed source
bindings, test-input audits, phase timings, proof reuse and repository gate results
are retained in `practical-assurance/consumer-resource-*` and `checkpoint-v120.json`.

Next audit the four active-goal completion requirements against the real public
network, exact bindings, dependency changes and separately reusable runtime
validation. Do not make arbitrary-target composition, general service/loader
qualification or full Hello/jq/DX-Ball activation prerequisites for this milestone.
Finite runtime evidence and conditional local proofs remain separate authorities.

Real consumer runtime validation (2026-09-14, v119): the retained PE32/Wine
fixture now executes both original caller slices and both ordinary-C consumers
through the existing generated cleanup machine/logical adapters. Thirteen cases
run on both sides (26 executions) using actual GlobalAlloc, GlobalFree, lstrlenA,
lstrcpyA, SetFocus and SendMessageA with two real EDIT windows. The logical adapter
uses its generated service-context layout; no new application proof engine or
production component API is introduced.

The cases exercise both bypass modes, unsigned mode/length boundaries, one/two
CRCRLF removals, empty/short text, allocation failure with empty/nonempty input,
interior text references, prefix/suffix guards, normal continuation frames and
ordered fault prefixes. Input aliases remain live through cleanup, scratch origins
expire after release, and input aliases expire at owner release. The EDIT subclass
forwards replacement to the real control, records current text, and returns selected
32-bit values including UINT32_MAX. A real focus callback changes the edit handle;
both callers subsequently observe current memory. The displayed UI contents remain
unchanged after mutation of the borrowed text and after its owner is freed.

Initial execution caught an incorrect caption origin in the fixture: caption is
in image section 1, while notice/window/edit words are in section 2. Correcting
that binding passes real view admission. Six deliberate controls now reject wrong
caption origin, a stale pre-cleanup handle read, missing native release registration,
a stale text generation, incorrect count arithmetic and a missing mode guard.

Seven new runtime tests and four existing cleanup-adapter tests pass in Nix; all
223 declared input paths match current bytes. The retained 27 local-proof tests
also still have all 25 declared inputs current. Four repository gates pass; the
boundary test retains exactly its two prior failures and seven unchanged offenders.
The strengthened runtime baseline takes 0.0352s preparation, 0.520s compilation,
0.0407s linking and 7.732s headless Wine execution, including process/display setup.
Existing public save and UI proof imports pass with model/header generation and
process execution forbidden (7.194s and 7.655s). Their engine files are unchanged;
no pilot or local proof query is rebuilt for this runtime work.

Evidence: `tests/fixtures/metapad-consumer-runtime/`,
`practical-assurance/consumer-runtime-test-inputs-v2.json`,
`consumer-runtime-native-v2.log`, `consumer-runtime-proof-reuse-v1.json`, and
`checkpoint-v119.json`. This is finite runtime validation, not universal caller
reachability, a replacement for local correctness proofs or activation authority.
Image/stack admission remains fixture-owned. Notice delivery is suppressed, so
the resource/message-box path still needs actual-service validation; the subclass
reply values are not claimed as default EDIT replies. Callback behavior outside
the tested focus update, service invocation failures and nonreturn remain outside
this fixture. Continue with those concrete applicability gaps and a requirement-by-
requirement milestone audit, preserving the distinction between trusted runtime
premises, their scoped validation, local proofs and strong qualification.

Public contract refinement (2026-09-14, v118): the real cleanup network now
supports checked withdrawal of normal-return frame guarantees and consumer-specific
proof dependencies. The existing composition product accepts `summaryExport` with
`normal-frame-subset-v1`. It can remove members of the checked frame conjunction;
it cannot add guarantees, alter premises, change outcomes or edit other contract
terms. The stronger proof domain and its nine queries remain unchanged, while the
exported contract and its evidence binding change.

The public experiment withdraws `state.edi==initial.edi`. UI replacement needs this
fact for its post-cleanup text argument. Save preparation overwrites EDI after
cleanup and instead needs ESI, EBX and EBP preserved. Each consumer model now
leaves every undeclared register arbitrary; complete original/C proofs establish
that the declared requirements suffice. All non-frame contract terms retain exact
semantic identity, including memory, aliases, lifetime, outcomes, environment and
progress. This is a checked conjunction rule, not compatibility inferred from a
signature or an arbitrary contract-subtyping implementation.

Through the actual Nix-backed public workflow, the supplier export changes in
20.782s using the same nine queries and zero model/compiler/solver runs. Save
reuses its exact proof key/files in 19.921s with zero such runs and a newly bound
supplier contract/evidence. UI returns incomplete in 10.437s, reports the exact
missing EDI guarantee, and does not reuse or generate a proof. Its source and
interface are unchanged. Both consumers expose required/available/missing facts
and provided/consumed contract identities in public JSON. The plain UI diagnostic
names the missing equality. This is a missing contract obligation, not evidence
that the unchanged implementation is wrong.

A deliberate underdeclaration control removes EDI from the UI profile itself;
the complete original/C proof then fails at native message arguments. This checks
that the required fact matters to real behavior. Other controls reject added DF
preservation, duplicate/noncanonical exports, changed stack behavior, stale proof
bytes and incorrect source edits. The refreshed save and UI public workflows both
still detect wrong edits and reuse repaired/compatible-neighbor proofs.

Evidence: `practical-assurance/public-cleanup-contract-refinement-v1/`,
`public-cleanup-composition-v14/`, `public-cleanup-save-v5/`,
`public-cleanup-replace-v2/` and `checkpoint-v118.json`. Twenty-seven targeted Nix

the subsequent export and implementation changes reused proofs as recorded.

The milestone remains active. It now has a real public contract-change and
selective-invalidation example as well as two consumer edit/reuse workflows.
Next establish and reuse the concrete caller/adapter/runtime premises, especially
the UI text/contents/lifetime and service assumptions, then audit the complete
network. Existing finite runtime checks remain scoped evidence. General contract
changes outside the implemented rule remain unsupported; strong qualification,
native activation and the broader Hello/jq/DX-Ball path are unchanged.

Two real consumer operations (2026-09-14, v117): the public workflow now
checks Metapad's guarded UI replacement operation as well as its save operation.
The UI boundary owns five transfers rooted at 0xb18e: both mode guards, cleanup,
argument setup and SendMessageA. All three structural incoming edges enter at
that root; this coverage does not establish actual caller reachability. Modes 2
and 3 bypass cleanup, and cleanup admission premises apply only on the call
branch. The ordinary C reads the edit-window handle after cleanup and uses a
separate fault tag, preserving every uint32 UI result including UINT32_MAX.

Both consumer models import the actual checked cleanup summary with both cleanup
bodies absent. The UI model checks complete view arguments, current public bytes
at both service boundaries, ordered fault prefixes, context preservation and
normal continuation transport. It assumes a named returning SendMessage relation;
text termination after cleanup, concrete UI behavior, callbacks, invocation
failures and nonreturning services remain unqualified. Corresponding post-memory
is not a NUL-termination guarantee.

The UI public baseline checks 2,763 properties in 2.514s; complete command latency
is 21.253s. An incorrect mode guard is rejected in 13.550s. Repair (18.228s) and a
compatible resource implementation change (19.009s) retain exactly the baseline
consumer proof key and files with zero model/compiler/solver runs. The resource
change propagates through cleanup with updated evidence. The save consumer was
rechecked after the shared checker change: its 1,992-property query takes 5.254s,
and both repair and neighbor proof reuse pass again. Neither experiment rebuilds
a pilot, native link or cleanup regional correctness proof.

A measured UI model change keeps the four disjoint private argument words outside
public-memory write history while checking every original push and argument.
The direct complete query drops from 84.981s to 2.405s under the same safety and
unwinding policy. The public optimized model is 864,729 bytes. This is an encoding
improvement for the same operation, not evidence that component size alone solves
proof performance. Public evidence import still costs about seven seconds.

Evidence: `practical-assurance/public-cleanup-replace-v1/`,
`practical-assurance/public-cleanup-save-v4/`, and `checkpoint-v117.json`.
Seventeen targeted Nix tests pass across four shards; all 23 declared input paths
match current bytes. Four repository gates pass. The boundary test retains its
two existing failures and seven unchanged size offenders. Negative controls cover
missing guards, wrong message arguments, ignored cleanup faults, a UI result/fault
collision, context corruption and a stale pre-cleanup handle read. Public text
keeps conditional correctness separate from runtime and activation authority.

The milestone remains open. These are two supported hand-defined operation
profiles, not general boundary-driven composition. Complete dependency contract
fingerprints are pinned; incompatible refinements reject before model construction
but have not been exercised as a public network refinement. Next establish and
reuse concrete caller/adapter/service applicability, then demonstrate which
consumers need rechecking after a meaningful contract change. Seven cleanup cuts
remain proof regions within one authoring component, not seven components.

Real save-consumer checkpoint (2026-09-14, v116): the existing public caller-check
product now compares an entire ordinary-C operation at Metapad 0x5c2a/0x5c34.
It invokes cleanup, then subtracts the returned removal count from the current
length word. All uint32 lengths remain admitted, including wrapping subtraction.
The actual outgoing two EBX pushes and EDI/ESI register values are checked as
continuation transport; the authored C does not expose those machine operations
as production APIs.

The cleanup composition now exports a complete conditional paired-operation
contract, with contract identity separate from implementation evidence. Its
original coverage includes all 35 owned transfers, and input correspondence
includes current bytes, complete views and the incoming DF bound. This summary
is consumed by the save proof without either cleanup implementation body. The
consumer overapproximates all public post-memory instead of assuming a narrow
callee write frame. Cleanup faults stop before the length update and pushes.
Caller writes before cleanup and extra normal-return writes reject, even if a
write is later restored. Caller service pointers, protocol state and reserved
context storage are preserved on both outcomes; a corrupting source edit rejects.
Concrete entry/runtime/service premises remain explicit.

Through `component check --source --local-contracts`, the real baseline checks

query files, zero save model/compiler/solver runs, and updated supplier evidence.
The neighbor command takes 18.624s; proof import alone does not eliminate command
and recursive evidence-validation overhead. Both evidence imports and reuse are
checked with processes and model/header generation forbidden.

Service-prefix composition checkpoint (2026-09-14, v115): the public phase now
binds the checked entry/loop/tail service models to complete ordered observation
prefixes. Final memory equality alone was insufficient: a temporary write before
a call could be restored before return. Entry now proves no public write events
before each of its three calls. The exact incoming-byte reader and shared world
initialization bind this stronger premise to call-time contents. Entry call order,
arguments and normalized results remain checked by the existing callbacks. The
loop has no service calls; tail records bind order, arguments, corresponding
results and the same arbitrary memory probe, including memory-fault prefixes.

The entry query checks 3,431 properties in 44.698s. Recording separate entry byte
snapshots had timed out at 600s; the no-write premise avoids that extra solver
state. Current loop/tail queries pass in 18.000s/170.224s using retained preparation
and exact slices. These regional rechecks follow a proof-engine change, not an
application-neighbor edit. No pilot is rebuilt.

The new body-free prefix query checks 12 properties in 0.233s in a 13,220-byte
model. Public baseline is 29.451s. A compatible resource implementation change
reuses both tail and composition proof files with zero consumer model/compiler/
solver runs in 19.665s. Evidence imports also forbid processes and model/header
generation. The operator displays the conditional service-prefix result while
retaining incomplete/non-authorizing whole-component status and unverified
runtime requirements. Evidence: `practical-assurance/public-cleanup-regions-v3/`
and `practical-assurance/public-cleanup-composition-v11/`.

The real recheck also exposed JSON-order sensitivity in the spatial reader: two
independent ESP/EBP initializations may appear in either order. Both exact
consecutive orders are now accepted; intervening effects still reject. Negative
controls cover temporary public writes, weakened fault scopes, hidden or moved
recorders, changed memory readers, dropped fault prefixes, reordered calls and
wrong call-time bytes. Setup bindings additionally prevent changed side selectors,
counter resets or probe initialization from bypassing those observations.

Validation: 28 targeted Nix tests pass in five shards; all 13 declared input

Next assemble the checked relations into a consumable conditional operation

Scoped control composition checkpoint (2026-09-14, v114): public composition
now consumes the actual guarded postconditions from entry, loop and tail, rather
than treating a matching property description as an unconditional guarantee.
The reader rejects weakened normal/back-edge scopes, extra post-call assumptions
or effects, and unsupported control. It collapses the complete seven-region
source graph into entry-to-loop, loop-to-loop and loop-to-tail continuations,
rejecting missing/overlapping ownership and crossings into a successor interior.

A 64-bit phase/cursor rank strictly decreases on every composed transition,
including fault termination. Segment-local termination uses the retained complete
regional queries and their unwinding assertions; dependency invocations must
return under the conditional contract. Blocking/diverging services are not
qualified. The saved-word mappings agree between entry and tail, and all loop
writes are disjoint from those words. Return-stack algebra restores caller SP+4;
the normal cursor/count domain excludes the fault sentinel. All seven properties
pass in 0.071s in a 14,642-byte model with application bodies absent.

The public baseline takes 27.168s. A compatible resource implementation change

with nine declared input paths matching current bytes. The boundary shard keeps

Production accessor relation checkpoint (2026-09-14, v113): public composition
now relates the actual returned-view helpers to the regional accessors under

A structural check binds this substitution to the complete owned cleanup graph:
149 reachable instructions, seven covered source regions, 11 accesses and eight
read-fault exits. Views are copied opaquely; descriptor-field observations,
reconstruction, unsupported widths and observable/cyclic read-fault continuations
reject. Scalar behavior changes still need their regional correctness proofs.
Expired origins, changed generation/coordinates, observing failed-read outputs
and admitting width three all have rejected controls. This profile is deliberately
explicit about supported source uses, not a claim that arbitrary C is parametric.

Complete adapter integration checkpoint (2026-09-14, v112): the existing native

miss this change: the included `portable-component.h` service table differs.
The full generated headers now match the current regional ABI.

Four new Nix integration tests pass in 21.881s, with all 218 declared input paths

C, complete production adapter, native allocation/reference functions and PE32
Wine allocation, length, copy, release and focus services. Empty/short strings,

and the repository fixture `tests/fixtures/metapad-cleanup-runtime/`.

Current-memory transport checkpoint (2026-09-14, v110): public composition now
binds all eight actual outgoing public-memory guarantees and successor premises
to a checked pointwise substitution rule. The successor's arbitrary input bytes
are instantiated with the predecessor's current contents at every physical
address. This forgets write history while preserving its resulting contents.
Both sides share that function, including physical aliases. The tail's zero
suffix is checked against the predecessor guarantee instead of being invented
at the cut. Missing guarantees, changed premises and altered readers reject;
negative controls expose unrelated fresh memory, a missing zero-suffix premise
and a one-byte overwrite beyond the suffix. A nonempty-domain check also passes.

The public query checks ten properties in 0.303s and compiles to 19,788 bytes.

baseline takes 24.960s; the compatible resource implementation change takes
18.504s and reuses both tail and composition proofs with zero model/compiler/
solver runs and identical proof files. Current products and phase timings are
retained under `practical-assurance/public-cleanup-composition-v5/`. Imports forbid
processes and model generation. The original regional queries and pilot are reused.
All 189 affected Nix tests pass across 25 shards, with all 789 declared input
paths matching current bytes. Four repository gates pass. The boundary check
retains exactly the two existing size failures and seven offenders. The affected
selection includes existing tests that declare all of `src`; their repeated
proof construction is separate from public supplier-edit reuse.

This discharges conditional current-memory/public-predicate transport for the
supported cuts. Descriptor/context lifetime, physical private-frame admission,

Private-object transport checkpoint (2026-09-14, v109): the public composition
phase now derives private-cell tables from the actual checked regional accessors.
It checks their correspondence with one 76-byte object containing arbitrary
current bytes. A fixed recipe compares scalar and byte stores using the same
arbitrary input value, preserves all other bytes and maintains the cell relation
after an arbitrary allowed store. This provides the inductive step for repeated
private updates without expanding application bodies or a global heap model.

The actual entry/loop/tail mappings preserve saved ESI/EBX/EBP, the return word,
the count and the loop's two byte temporaries inside a tail word. Read/write
widths, guard identity, writable cells and direct service-adapter writes are
bound; unsupported cell effects, escaping cell addresses and premature narrowing
reject. Negative controls catch lost private bytes, wrong coordinates, incoherent
overlapping cells and a corrupted scalar store against independent byte writes.

This closes conditional private-cell/byte transport for the supported operation,
not complete state composition or the authoring-component network. Physical

Composition admission checkpoint (2026-09-14, v108): a new public composition
phase imports the current entry/loop/tail receipts and extracts spatial premises
from their actual proved models. Its model contains no application bodies. Entry
admission entails the loop's spatial requirements but not the tail's six spatial
requirements. The checked register transport changes the stack anchor by -12;
the tail needs entry-relative private storage `[-72,+4)`, whereas entry models
only `[-40,+4)`, and excludes the complete image rather than only the length IAT.

An explicit proposed envelope discharges all spatial implications. Its four
caller requirements cover private capacity and private/text/image separation;
two allocator guarantees cover future scratch placement against private storage
and the image. Each omission has a counterexample, and a separate query proves
the proposed domain nonempty. These are unqualified requirements, not silently
added caller assumptions or a proof of preserved memory/lifetime.

Both public CLI/Nix cases have the expected incomplete operation status. Baseline

Typed editable-tail checkpoint (2026-09-14, v105): a host C type check

The strengthened actual tail passes all 4,405 properties in 136.029s, with both

The compiled source-footprint checker covers the real tail's byte helpers, three

The checker stays with the real experiment in `tests/unit/components/cleanup_tail_frame.py`

Four compiled admission tests pass in the current Nix shard, with all six

Public boundary refinement checkpoint (2026-09-14, v104): the existing

`component-source-region-check.nix` now accepts `solver = "cadical"` as well as

## Immediate milestone: a real hand-defined component network (2026-09-13)

The operator reset the goal around genuine independence. Use retained Metapad
inputs to demonstrate meaningful application components with a loop, allocation
or mutable shared memory, multiple calling contexts and multi-level composition.
Each local proof must use its own original behavior and explicit dependency
contracts, with neighboring implementation bodies absent. Check caller/adapter
compatibility separately. Do not make another large-model solver experiment a
prerequisite for implementing this boundary.

Completion requires ordinary-C editing, detected violations, repair and contract
refinement through the public workflow. A requalified neighbor that preserves its
complete contract must leave the consumer proof reusable without recompiling its
model or solving it again. An incompatible contract change must identify and
invalidate affected consumers. Inspect actual model contents and build dependencies,
measure the complete edit cycle, and retain exact implementation/evidence binding.
Conditional runtime contracts require independent validation; bounded checks,
path witnesses, isolated proof adapters and source-only frame theorems do not
satisfy the real application-correctness milestone. Boundary recommendations
remain manual. Strong network, pilot, native-link, export and portability exits
remain longer-term requirements, not prerequisites for this demonstration.

All seven public Nix-backed cases have their expected outcomes, and their
current evidence imports with model generation and subprocesses forbidden.
Revision 3 checks 1,880 properties in 20.023s (27.874s public latency), including
all eight outgoing guarantees. A wrong byte store fails in 26.431s; repair reuses
the exact model and proof files in 6.904s with zero model/compiler/inventory/solver
runs. Outside-loop edits similarly reuse in 9.874s; this is regional reuse, not
checked neighboring-component compatibility. The seven products have retained
Nix roots under `practical-assurance/public-region-check-v7/`. Seven focused
revision-3 tests and all 12 integrated region tests pass; the latter's seven
declared inputs (including the complete source directory) match current bytes.
Four repository gates pass. The boundary test retains exactly the two existing
size failures and seven offending files/line counts. Both affected Nix runs are
still active and are not yet claimed complete.

Supplier-bound cleanup tail checkpoint (2026-09-14, v103): the complete retained
14-unit tail now passes all 4,295 properties under explicit local contracts,
including both allocation outcomes. The existing SAT backend takes 115.637s;
Z3 times out at 180s on the identical compiled model. All language-safety and

a public whole-component receipt or runtime qualification.

The actual resource supplier and its byte-reading loop edit are rechecked through
`component check --source --local-contracts` in 10.234s and 9.128s. Their checked
transition domains agree; distinct implementation receipts remain bound and both

reference, clobbers and private-stack frame. It materializes the adapter return
word and allows all declared child private writes, rather than silently preserving
them. Neither original nor authored supplier body appears in the consumer model.
Both supplier receipts can be validated against the consumed domain without
compiler or solver execution; this is still an experimental consumer import,
not the requested public network edit/reuse workflow.

The sparse runtime can bypass history for explicitly preserved physical spans,
but every recorded write must prove the corresponding frame. Independent byte-array
checks cover overlapping source/service writes, aliases, omitted-frame negatives
and the PE32 address limit. Read-only view permissions never imply this invariant,
and incoming bytes remain arbitrary current contents. The real tail uses this
rule for the module/window/notice fields. Its benefit has not been isolated from
the solver change. Editing the actual resource ID from 31 to 32 passes compiled
source transport and fails the expected call-argument obligation in 47.824s.

Composition inspection also found that entry/loop constructed read-only text
descriptors while the tail requires writable copy-back. Both producers now pass
with the tail's complete writable descriptor: entry checks 3,418 properties in
52.322s and the loop 1,880 in 12.080s. Their actual cleanup bodies and observers
are unchanged. These experimental variants do not silently change public runtime
contract revision 2. Evidence is under `practical-assurance/cleanup-descriptor-v1/`
and `practical-assurance/cleanup-tail-supplier-sat-v1/`.

Still required: checked public entry/loop/tail receipt composition; admission of
the tail's image/private/public separation and lifetimes; complete source-effect
admission for editable tail candidates; concrete copy/free/resource/message/focus
service compatibility, including result conventions, string validity and callback
effects; and the full multi-level public edit/refine/neighbor-reuse demonstration.
The three proof regions remain parts of one cleanup authoring component.

Terminal transport and allocation-failure refinement (2026-09-14, v102):
the actual 14-unit cleanup tail now has compiled source correspondence, including
its normal result expression and five early returns. A terminal region needs
only its entry marker; no synthetic exit observer or production API is required.
The matcher still compares return expressions, effects, storage and all control
edges. Models with synthetic cut ordinals retain their tagged-outcome restriction.
This transport remains non-authorizing and does not qualify service behavior.

The tail exposed a missing incoming invariant: the generic loop domain permits
null scratch with a nonzero removed count and no pending bytes, reaching copy-back
without a live source. The retained counterexample now includes its actual input
assignments. A refinement says that null scratch implies either input zero or
pending CR/LF bytes at input and input+1 within the text span. Entry establishes
this fact (3,418 complete checks), and the loop preserves it at both normal exits
along with all seven public-memory domain predicates (1,880 complete checks).
The actual compiled cleanup and observers remain unchanged. Evidence and input
audits are under `practical-assurance/cleanup-contract-refinement-v1/`.

The combined refined tail query timed out at both 60 and 180 seconds. It has no
passing correctness receipt. Exhaustively splitting allocation failure and
success proves all 4,140 properties in the null case in 22.570s; the live case
still times out at 180s. Both cases retain all assertions and pass actual compiled
source transport. Evidence is under `practical-assurance/cleanup-tail-allocation-cases-v1/`;
one passing case cannot establish the whole tail. The refinement is experimental: public runtime
contract revision 2 remains unchanged, and automatic receipt composition is not
implemented for it. Private frames, image separation, allocation/lifetime and
copy/resource/message/focus compatibility still require explicit admission and
composition. Engine edits invalidate the older public producer binding; v101's
reuse measurements are historical, not a fresh import against this engine.
An additional live-case query reuses call-argument and current-memory facts only
after asserting them, retaining all earlier proof obligations. It also times out
at 180s (`cleanup-tail-live-lemmas-v1`); no proof or speedup is claimed. The next
consumer work must resolve the live tail and import the actual resource supplier,
not accumulate more passing null-case variants.

The entry experiment's source footprint check rejects unmodeled context/parameter

and production-lint gates pass; the repository boundary recheck retains exactly

compose complete meaningful application regions, rather than add more variants
of this single-call projection. Strong qualification remains separate.

Authored caller binding checkpoint (2026-09-13, v94): the public source-check
product now accepts manual `sourceCallRegions` anchors around an actual authored
service call. It compiles the complete ordinary and marked source through the

and the existing structural cut checker establish the selected region; a narrow
projection accounts for every declaration, call, result copy and temporary
lifetime end. The result identifies the actual service, arguments, context type
and live result local. Extra effects, branches and stale source/model or projection
metadata cannot silently inherit that correspondence.

For retained Metapad `cleanup.c`, this binds the ID-31 resource call and its live

construction takes 0.059s and compilation 0.110s. The second original context

caller-cut result does not establish another authored component.

The Nix-backed public preparation takes 4.742s for baseline, 5.068s for the changed

The reuse audit also imports the valid extended private-stack domain and marks

Validation: 132 targeted Nix tests pass, including the authored caller proof and

This is progress toward public caller proofs, not their completed integration.

Conditional caller-cut checkpoint (2026-09-13, v93): a production reader now

Stale original/object bytes, changed runtime claims and incomplete property
inventories are rejected without compiler or solver execution.

Two real Metapad call regions, 0x5646 and 0x570e, now pass a complete conditional
paired check using that supplier transition with neither supplier body compiled.
The model checks IDs 31/32, universally related declared readable inputs, arbitrary

pass in 0.352s checker time. Wrong IDs, damaged live stack words, an omitted call
and changed input memory restored after the call are rejected. A temporary change
outside the supplier's readable views is accepted when restored. These are proof
regions, not complete caller components; their ordinary C fragments still need
binding to the authored component through the public cut workflow.

The refreshed public supplier workflow passes baseline and loop edit in 11.376s

Validation: 133 targeted Nix tests pass with all 731 realized source inputs matching

Native image adapter checkpoint (2026-09-13, v92): the generated logical caller
now has tests using the actual native image locator, resolver and realizer with

The retained 32-bit checks bind generated C, compiled models, tools and raw query

using checked supplier transitions next; its current-string invariant remains a

This advances the public manual-boundary and ordinary-edit workflow. The remaining

The aggregate selection preserves all 1,047 original property identities and adds

The consumer retains the earlier bounded checking policy, including disabled

A local adapter implementation rewrite satisfies the unchanged complete contract

The generic whole-registry snapshot boundary did not qualify: pointer and by-value

lookup/realization contract. Initial admission requires fewer than 17 records;

both cuts in 66.585s. All four original metadata/context predicates pass together
in 16.256s. Assertion coverage reaches the actual `tail:text` context check on
that unchanged GOTO in 79.420s. Only the three corresponding header call sites
change; application C, input relations, helper implementation and trust identities
are unchanged. Exact scoped header recovery and raw coverage/query binding are
included in `practical-assurance/real-reference-continuity-audit-v1.json`.

This demonstrates reuse of one proposed runtime invariant at two actual outgoing
cuts, not reuse of a neighboring application-component proof. The complete run
of the preceding one-cut model finds tail text metadata/context and the
`suppress_notice` context unresolved at 180s. Its audited terminal inventory is
57/391 authored assertions satisfied, three unresolved and 331 unexecuted;
all 27 safety groups pass, covering 23,795 static properties and unwinding.
The run takes 1,902.426s. Entry nonvacuity and the continuity helper's return
are reached in 61.271s and 131.733s. Audit:
`practical-assurance/real-reference-continuity-region-audit-v1.json`.
The two-cut model has only selected qualification so far. These runs overlap and
cannot establish an isolated whole-region or public-edit speedup. Neither the
one-cut inventory nor its safety results transfer automatically to the new model.

Explicit continuity experiment (2026-09-12): observing actual selected-origin
issuance and lifetime/bounds/address still times out at 180s; actual realization
reachability passes in 104.840s. A new experimental trusted contract,
`admitted-reference-continuity` revision 2, instead checks descriptor/context,
address, allocation metadata and private/exposed access scope at use. Its
applicability pair passes in 38.891s and the selected original metadata/context
predicates pass in 14.119s. This deliberately assumes repeated resolution and
realization guarantees; their 0.020s solver phase is not an independent proof of
those guarantees. Preparation/compilation take 0.010s/0.503s. Revision 1 omitted
private/exposed scope and must not be promoted. Exact source deltas, inputs,
contract identities and raw query evidence are audited in
`practical-assurance/real-reference-continuity-audit-v1.json`.

Independent bounded checks execute current generated native allocation/reference
and proof origin bodies with the Win32 ABI, without selecting conditional runtime
contracts. Eleven scenarios cover repeated resolution/registration, an appended
neighbor origin, interior aliases, changed current bytes, failed release,
successful release/address reuse, malformed permissions/extent, changed private
scope, and internal allocation exhaustion. Offsets and lower/upper-address
placements vary symbolically. All assertions and safety/unwind checks pass in
14.275s; actual wrong-address and removed-lifetime-guard mutations fail in
15.002s/15.148s. Named scenario coverage passes in 18.039s. The first harness's
eleven covers in `main` were all reached but failed the reader's one-witness-per-
function inventory; the corrected harness preserves the scenarios and assumptions.
Audit: `practical-assurance/reference-continuity-native-audit-v1.json`.

These are bounded internal-runtime checks, not original-binary validation or
qualification of the real consumer's selector, exposed-range invalidation,
admission/issuer enforcement and production lowering. Output variables in the
fixture are fresh; arbitrary output aliasing is not covered. The experimental
identity remains rejected by production readers. The complete-region diagnostic
above retains every original application requirement and removes only an unused,
unassumed whole-origin diagnostic assertion. Small runtime tests overlap
that run, so its elapsed time cannot establish an isolated speedup. No public
edit/repair/refinement/neighbor-proof reuse or activation authority follows.

Outgoing reference-frame comparison (2026-09-12): at both actual text

Consuming only the three passing facts at their qualification sites retains all

The actual metadata/context pair also remains incomplete: 180.218s for the

A second, explicitly unqualified experiment caches the pure world-reference
lookup by world identity, selector, arguments and allocation metadata. It keeps

That candidate also times out at 180.168s, increasing symbolic steps from

Next boundary: express the selected reference's issuance and live-object
invariant through the existing issued-access and namespace interfaces. The pure
lookup and the wrapper that registers origins have different effects. Preserve
the selected identity, extent, permissions, lifetime and bytes while allowing

Before implementation promotion, measure a consumer that actually omits or
reuses the repeated lookup/registration reasoning, with its applicability and
effects checked. Additional facts in the intact call chain and ordinary guarded
memoization have now failed the selected performance comparison. The three
qualified frame facts and mutation control remain reusable prerequisites, not

neighbor-reuse and long-term strong network/native/export obligations stay open.

Actual-call transport and read-body omission comparison (2026-09-12): the retained

The candidate replaces that source read with the existing current-byte getter
and removes the actual runtime reader function from the compiled caller. Its

The narrower independent frame model passes all fourteen selected read-frame
and projection assertions. Widening each read's observation bit independently

Sequencing decision: do not spend another full qualification cycle polishing
this wrapper substitution on the strength of body absence alone. The measured
comparison does not justify it as the performance boundary. Inspect and factor
the repeated metadata/context and memory/reference predicates still retained

Retain the transport result and negative control as prerequisites for later
composition. Complete real-region assurance and public edit/violation/repair,
contract refinement and neighboring business-proof reuse remain the acceptance
milestone; the small real component network remains open.

This completes validation of the instrumented entry/read probe. Its query work

do not consume these entry facts after intervening effects without that proof.
The real application's fourteen-fact regional run remains incomplete below.

The matched V15 public run still times out on the individual tail-alignment query

The large batch is not evidence that every individual state assertion is hard.
Applying the existing batching helper to the unchanged seven-fact model produces

This experiment uses the existing grouping rule, not the complete production
runner or its recursive timeout subdivision. All four subsequently pass

changing their semantics. Evidence: `practical-assurance/real-outgoing-state-batches-audit-v1.json`
and `real-outgoing-memory-singletons-audit-v1.json`.

The remaining-state runner has returned after recursive subdivision: nine of

The newer memory decomposition above resolves the selected tail-text assertion
in a different diagnostic model; do not combine these inventories into a complete

introduced by these diagnostic experiments.

Complete public repair, contract refinement, actual native applicability and

The earlier decoder-only checkpoint below remains a valid narrower measurement;

Real parameter-decoder summary experiment (2026-09-12): the actual application
model now supplies a narrower candidate than an opaque successful-entry summary.
Its adapter repeats five reference resolutions already performed by input setup.
Fifteen added assertions check the exact returned status/reference, unchanged
source world and unchanged issued-origin table at those actual call sites.
All pass: result checks take 41.847s, world frames 11.772s and origin frames
36.522s. Recompiling the unmodified retained application reproduces its exact
GOTO digest before instrumentation. The candidate substitution returns those
established references and retains stack-owned view construction, runtime
callback identities, ordinary C, cut transport and existing input assumptions.
This is a diagnostic substitution, not an implemented production summary reader
or complete substituted-model qualification. Actual native applicability,
public edit/refine/neighbor reuse and strong activation remain open.

The matched control measurement does not support promoting this narrow decoder
boundary into a production feature: both the unchanged application and the
substitution time out at 180.239s/180.237s. Symbolic steps decrease from 368,887

an interactive workflow. Keep this diagnostic as evidence and avoid another
decoder-specific production framework. The next boundary experiment must address
the remaining shared input-memory and cut-state construction, with explicit
current bytes, aliases, live generations and failure outcomes. Measure complete

The entry receipt also needs an explicit outcome distinction: its relation

assumptions. Previously issued origin entries remain unchanged and the count
does not decrease (102.020s). These are additional scoped observations, not a
general guarantee of successful initialization. The initial mixed byte-array/
struct snapshot observer was invalid: a small unchanged-state fixture reproduces
its failure. Typed snapshots pass that fixture and detect a changed final field;
the original frame failure is not evidence of changed application memory.
The corrected real world/machine frame query passes in 7.531s. Deliberately
corrupting the real decoder's returned extent, source-world write count and
issued-origin lifetime each fails its corresponding local assertion in
14.358s, 13.486s and 16.692s. These are summary-observer negative controls in
the retained proof model, not new native-runtime validation or a public
contract-violation/repair demonstration. The audit verifies the original compiled
models, raw query hashes, all fifteen selected assertions, identical matched
query arguments, unchanged application roots and the exact five substitutions.
No production Python, pilot rebuild or native link changes in this checkpoint.
Evidence is under `practical-assurance/real-entry-summary-observations-v2/`,
`real-entry-summary-observations-v3/`, `entry-observer-fixture-v1/`,
`real-decoder-summary-v1/`, `real-decoder-negatives-v1/` and
`real-decoder-summary-audit-v1.json`.

Qualified real loop entry and public implementation edit (2026-09-12): the
unchanged Metapad loop entry now completes under the four registered runtime
contracts. Increasing the entry budget from two to 60 seconds completed safety,

20,670-property safety inventory, its unwinding checks and nonvacuity. It took
544.504s, executing 96 new entry processes and reusing 62. Entry GOTO bytes,

across these budget retries. The longest completed assertion checks were

This is completion under a larger budget, not a new implementation speedup.

A real public ordinary-C edit reverses the branch structure around the loop's
first read, retaining one callback invocation and the same success/failure
behavior. The source package is rebuilt through its existing producer. Only
its C file and source manifest change. Public preparation confirms a changed
application GOTO model and identical extracted entry GOTO model; compiled-prefix
correspondence is checked again. All 158 entry processes are reused, with zero
fresh entry queries, and the complete command takes 49.572s. This demonstrates
complete conditional entry-proof reuse after a local implementation edit.
It does not demonstrate neighboring business-component proof reuse or complete
application-edit assurance: both runs retain two-second application budgets
and return incomplete with five boundary requirements and activation=false.

The larger-budget public application check now reuses all 158 entry processes,
passes its 22,949-property safety inventory and nonvacuity, but control equivalence
still exceeds 180 seconds. Complete command time is 384.339s. Its application
GOTO model matches the prior baseline exactly. The timeout transcript ends in

whole timeout measured solver time. Evidence: `public-loop-application-audit-v1.json`.

Inspection confirms a remaining integration gap: the execution engine qualifies
the separate entry model, then checks the original application GOTO model without
an entry-summary substitution. Entry reuse therefore does not remove construction
from each application query. Next establish and consume a checked entry result/frame
relation for this real boundary, covering values, aliases, live view owners, current
memory, reference issuance and effects. Reuse existing relations and transport;

fault and caller obligations while measuring whether this changes the actual
application formula. Actual native permission/caller/content/lifetime applicability,
detected application violation and repair, contract refinement and the full
component network remain open.
No production Python changed in this checkpoint and no pilot rebuild or native
link was run. Evidence: `practical-assurance/loop-entry-budget-audit-v1.json`,
`public-loop-branch-edit-audit-v1.json` and their referenced public engine packets.

Real loop fault triage and entry repair (2026-09-12): the retained conditional
control query was partitioned after both executions, without admitting a new
input restriction. Return and other non-memory-fault outcomes pass in 10.542s
and 10.639s; memory-fault outcomes remain unresolved at 90.211s. Further splits
by whether the source reached the cut, and by text-view/image-view/body-return
fault sites, each remain unresolved at 90 seconds. This does not identify a

experiment adds diagnostic observer state only. Its inherited scope prose and
original-harness hash label are corrected in the separate audit; no coverage
claim relies on those labels. Some queries overlapped small compiler jobs.

The actual extracted entry's generated owner-pointer identity is repaired in
production: declare its storage at the common BEGIN and assign it at the same
checked cut location. Descriptor validation, owner equality, lifetime checks,
ordinary C and the compiled-prefix checker remain unchanged. This avoids a
lexical identity change when a nested cut is extracted. A compiler regression
uses the generated header, accepts nested-cut extraction and rejects a changed
owner assignment. All 38 tests across four selected Nix shards pass, with no skips.

The real public conditional loop command now passes compiled-prefix comparison:
483 instruction pairs and 287 common helpers. It runs 63 fresh entry processes,
reuses none, and remains incomplete under the deliberate two-second budgets;

A pinned-client recovery takes 2.24 seconds without rerunning the proof; the
current reader independently validates the retained receipt. These are failed
attempt and cached recovery costs, not a successful cold public latency claim.
Subsequent experiment clients and their engines both use immutable snapshots.

The live measurement shows why query order matters: descriptor assertions run
before application exit observations under the old V13 order. Conditional SAT
checks now select V14 application-first scheduling: exit control/value and
observable memory/calls/atomics precede transported memory and descriptor
metadata. Every assertion, language-safety gate, unwind check, input world and
exact evidence binding remains required. V11--V13 receipts retain their own
orders; ordinary strong and SMT defaults remain unchanged. Focused tests show an
actual application counterexample before a controlled metadata timeout, while a
passing observable check remains incomplete if metadata cannot be checked. A
full legacy-to-V14 replay passes both readers and imports only identical queries.
Fifty targeted Nix tests and four repository checks pass.

The pinned public comparison `practical-assurance/public-consumer-application-first-v1/`
returned the counterexample and audited counts recorded above.
The deliberate post-cut wrong-byte control is prepared and has not run. Neither
faster real fault detection nor complete local edit latency is established yet;
metadata/transport qualification and the real public repair/refinement/neighbor
workflow remain acceptance work. No pilot or native link is rebuilt.

Earlier audited public entry qualification and edit reuse (2026-09-12): the real Metapad
baseline and actual counter-loop edit both pass the complete conditional entry
check. The baseline command takes 620.15 seconds, with 121 fresh entry processes
and 50 reused from the preceding partial run; this is not a cold benchmark.
The edited command takes 50.19 seconds and reuses all 171 entry processes with
zero fresh entry queries. The paired model changes while the entry model,
preparation binding and compiled prefix relation remain identical. No pilot
rebuild or native link runs. All 259 assertion sites, safety and nonvacuity are
bound by `practical-assurance/entry-qualified-audit-v2.json`.

Both component checks remain incomplete, with five boundary requirements and
eleven deferred regions. The ordinary query budget was two seconds, independently
of the 180-second entry budget. The 50.19-second command is not complete component
edit latency or neighboring business-proof reuse. Current targeted Nix validation
passes 33 tests without skips and four repository checks. The next action is to
use this completed entry evidence while checking the actual consumer effects at
a meaningful budget, then exercise violation, repair and contract refinement.
Do not repeat isolated entry qualification or increase component counts as a
substitute for the real network workflow.

Real consumer continuation (2026-09-12): checking the unchanged actual edited
region with a 30-second ordinary budget takes 676.25 seconds. All 171 entry
processes are reused, with no fresh entry queries. The ordinary model is unchanged;
its 38 safety processes contain 15 successes and 23 exhausted parent/child
attempts. It stops at the singleton
`__CPROVER_spx_checked_runtime_write.pointer_dereference.1`; nonvacuity also
times out at 30 seconds, so authored application assertions have not run.
This is incomplete evidence, not a failed equivalence claim. The audit retains
all output bindings and the already-compiled 2,013,059-byte consumer model:
`practical-assurance/consumer-qualified-entry-audit-v1.json`.

That exact pointer query passes in a retained-model diagnostic in 36.79 seconds
with a 180-second budget. Verbose timings show 13.58 seconds symbolic execution,
3.48 seconds SSA conversion and 16.04 seconds SAT solving. There is no compilation
and no semantic change; this single diagnostic does not qualify the region.
The public continuation `practical-assurance/public-consumer-qualified-entry-v2/`
uses the completed entry and partial consumer evidence at 180 seconds per query.
Its completed result and subsequent V14 counterexample are recorded above.
No pilot rebuild or native link runs.

Earlier retained real entry and edit replay (2026-09-12): the unchanged Metapad
entry model now passes all 259 authored assertion sites, every safety partition
and nonvacuity under the four existing named runtime contracts. The final
180-second-budget continuation takes 417.78 seconds, executes 101 processes and
reuses 40 from earlier continuations. That is continuation cost, not cold proof
latency. Reference-address validity passes in 120.43 seconds. No additional
span lemma, solver policy or runtime contract is adopted.

The actual edited public model then reuses all 141 entry processes with fresh
solver execution forbidden, in 0.91 seconds. Its entry bytes, preparation binding
and prefix relation are unchanged; its paired model differs. This measurement
covers retained entry revalidation and excludes compilation, Nix, other regions
and full component qualification. Evidence:
`practical-assurance/retained-entry-budget-v3/` and
`practical-assurance/retained-entry-edit-replay-v1/`.

The public conditional checker now accepts `--entry-query-timeout` independently
of `--query-timeout`. The default still inherits the ordinary query budget;
explicit values must be positive integers and require conditional assurance.
This changes scheduling only, and entry timing records expose their budget.
The Nix/provider forwarding and engine budget isolation have targeted coverage.
The public baseline/edit integration run uses ordinary queries at two seconds
and entry queries at 180 seconds; the audited results are recorded above.

The first public integration attempt exposed an older evidence-retention bug:
when coverage failed, the ordinary shard published its coverage result and lost
the earlier property result. Raw property files remained, but importing their
unpublished inventories correctly failed exact-output binding. Conditional
shards now retain and validate both results while preserving the original
aggregate status. Legacy packets without property evidence offer only their
published coverage to the cache; property queries run fresh. The exact reader's
corruption and unbound-output rejection rules are unchanged. A forced coverage
timeout regression checks both new reuse and the legacy fallback. The failed
Nix workspace was removed; the rerun enables failed-build retention.

Independent entry queries and public edit reuse (2026-09-11): the existing
partitioned assertion, language-safety and nonvacuity engines now check the
separate entry after compiled-prefix correspondence matches. The conditional
reader binds its actual model, root, tools, recipes, runtime contracts and output
evidence. The complete compiled assertion-site manifest must match CBMC
discovery: the real entry has 259 sites with 221 distinct descriptions. Repeated
labels do not lose site coverage. Only complete entry evidence sets qualification
flags; ordinary component queries still use their original models. Entry
qualification alone does not qualify the component, its callers or activation.

The real public counter edit preserves the entry model and prefix relation while
changing the paired model. It reuses 46 completed safety queries and four
inventories, and executes 51 fresh entry queries; the baseline executes 89.
Complete public commands take 623.98/275.02 seconds at an eight-second query
limit. Both remain incomplete, with five boundary requirements and eleven
deferred regions. This is measured partial process reuse, not neighboring
business-component proof reuse or complete edit latency. No pilot or native
link is rebuilt. Entry diagnostics retain about 295/293 MB including query
evidence; compiler inventories alone still cost about 195 MB.

The baseline discovers 21,638 safety sites and exhausts safety subdivision
before authored assertions run. A retained-model continuation with a 30-second
query limit passes all safety partitions and four assertion queries, then times
out on `spx-bisimulation-view-reference-byte-index`;
nonvacuity also remains incomplete. It takes 135.56 seconds with no compilation.
Its working directory differs from Nix, so exact cache binding correctly causes
35 fresh queries and zero reuse. This is a diagnostic, not a public speedup
comparison. The eight-second budget was too close to query preparation cost;
the last buffered message did not establish which solver phase exhausted time.
A subsequent verbose unchanged-model query passes in 45.91 seconds: 4.86 seconds
symbolic execution, 2.13 seconds SSA conversion and 37.82 seconds SAT solving,
with 191,100 symbolic steps, 3.01 million variables and 11.56 million clauses.
The dominant measured cost is solving, not SSA conversion.

The 90-second retained continuation reuses 33 processes and executes ten. It
passes byte-index, scratch native-view admission, input-owner, alignment and
escape checks, and obtains a nonvacuity witness in 54.97 seconds. The full entry
remains incomplete because `spx-bisimulation-view-reference-address` times out
at 90 seconds. The complete continuation takes 380.16 seconds, including failed
parent assertion groups and their successful subdivision. This is still local
entry evidence, not component qualification or a public workflow completion.

Two same-model experiments do not justify a policy change: Z3 exhausts 60 seconds
on byte-index, and aggregating every authored assertion exhausts 90 seconds.
An explicit checked span-bound consequence proves byte-index in 35.20 seconds
but adds a separate 45.18-second lemma check. No overall benefit is established;
the candidate stays outside production. Its baseline reproduces the public GOTO
bytes exactly. Evidence: `practical-assurance/entry-explicit-span-v1/`,
`entry-byte-index-baseline-v1/`, `entry-byte-index-smt-v1/`,
`entry-all-assertions-v1/` and `retained-entry-budget-v2/`.

A current small fixture passes all 66 entry assertions, safety and nonvacuity;
its repeat reuses all 51 entry processes with fresh CBMC execution forbidden.
Full safety checking exposed a missing coverage-observer body. The harness now
supplies a no-op body using CBMC's native Boolean type, preserving argument
effects and missing-callee checks elsewhere. Seven targeted Nix shards pass
51 tests without skips, and metadata, architecture, lint and format checks pass.
Audit: `practical-assurance/entry-queries-audit-v1.json`; continuation:
`practical-assurance/retained-entry-budget-v1/result.json`.

The preceding reference-address blocker is discharged by the complete entry

Earlier public compiled-prefix correspondence (2026-09-11): the conditional checker now
binds the restricted structural relation to both actual compiled models, their

exposes `prefix_correspondence` separately from still-unchecked source conformance

Both retained real counter variants match: 464 instruction pairs, all 43 unknown
branches, two false-assumption exits and one guarded frontier. All 289 other
compiled functions and static state match. The checker preserves exact local
identities, declarations, havoc, effects, assumptions, types and failed-pointer
object bindings. Removed automatic objects/types cannot be referenced by the
matched prefix or retained environment. Silent cycles reject. Only the existing
restricted discarded-parameter prelude is erased; dereferences remain unsupported.
This is a restricted correspondence rule, not a general source slicer.

The ordinary public model was reproduced byte for byte. It leaves startup and
initialization generation to CBMC's explicit query entry point. Independent entry
compilation now follows the same convention, so comparison checks the actual
public models without ignoring a startup mismatch. Baseline and edited entry
GOTO bytes and relation digests are identical, while their paired models differ.
Public structural comparison takes 2.21/1.92 seconds after inventory extraction.
Complete diagnostic commands take 50.50/48.93 seconds with a two-second query
limit; they remain incomplete with five boundary requirements and eleven deferred
regions. These are not complete proof/edit latency benchmarks.
Full compiler-inventory retention also costs about 195 MB per entry variant;
that storage cost remains a scaling limitation.

The public entry's selected escape assertion passes in 7.14 seconds. The preceding

Next: bind independent entry assertion/safety/coverage inventories and existing

The restricted profile supports a BEGIN before stateful source operations;

The retained real Metapad baseline and actual counter edit now pass this

Entry isolation and construction-order correction (2026-09-11): a restricted

The original selected entry predicates pass in 102.68/95.78 seconds and

World reset eagerly cached stack bytes before initial-memory facts were
installed. Production generation now leaves that cache empty when memory facts

The corrected real entry passes construction order in 7.65 seconds and

Next: implement checked source-entry conformance and separate entry-model

A subsequent event witness computes the entry predicate and invalidates the

The existing incoming-cut relation separately establishes the selected initial

The entry-only model already has 192,522 symbolic steps and about 12 million SAT

A real local counter rewrite after the allocated cut also changes the compiled

Current boundary-refinement result (2026-09-11): the real allocated cut does
not establish scratch/text separation. A counterexample admits a two-byte text

in 21.70 seconds; its exact/source terminator checks remain unresolved. Do not
infer fresh storage from a valid reconstructed view or treat the entire problem
as solver performance.

The existing `bytes_address` invariant now supports local native views through

transport, reject a valid interior-view move that breaks the address invariant,
and reject a forged generation even when the address expression agrees. All
21 tests in the two targeted Nix shards pass without skips; lint, format,
metadata and architecture checks pass.

A hand-authored proposal adds overflow-safe scratch/text separation to all eight

A separate law checks current-byte preservation through the actual retained
writer for arbitrary disjoint one-to-four-byte stores and admitted histories
in 20.00 seconds; the overlapping-store control fails in 0.90 seconds. It is a

invariant, avoiding a redundant reference translation. Its selected separation

A checked incoming-address normalization passes its guard in 18.25 seconds but
does not complete the original compound assertion. The proposed cuts must not be
treated as established invariants until their predecessors, nonvacuity and
preservation obligations pass. Application source,
original component inputs, runtime contract identities and supplier evidence
are retained; the internal cut domain is explicitly strengthened by the proposal.
Audits: `practical-assurance/disjoint-cut-refinement-audit-v1.json` and
`practical-assurance/refined-cut-consumer-audit-v1.json`. The latter binds the

A latest-write selector is equivalent to the retained byte reader in an

This diagnostic is not integrated. Continue with composition of a live-view

Further equivalent lookup rearrangements or extra cut counts are not evidence
of independent assurance. See `practical-assurance/byte-write-selection-audit-v1.json`.

Latest issued-reference experiment (2026-09-11): a diagnostic cache checks the
full resolver footprint before reusing a successful resolution. The original
text-metadata assertion passes in **32.17 seconds**; the text-extent assertion
still times out at 120.04 seconds. No application assertion is removed and no
application assumption is added. This is an unregistered candidate, not an
admitted summary or proof of whole-region performance.

Seventeen bounded scenarios compare status, output, origin updates and world
frames against the original full proof resolver in **6.37 seconds**. Separate
coverage confirms all seventeen reach the final comparisons. They include failed
and successful release, address reuse, actual/native generation changes, extent,
private/exposed ranges, conflicting/appended origins, byte writes, null/one-past
and integer-edge requests. Removing the actual lifetime-generation guard gives
the intended counterexample in **4.15 seconds**. These checks use the retained
three-allocation model; they are not arbitrary-state or full native-runtime
validation. The arbitrary-state preservation law times out with both SAT and

times out; exact repair compiles in 0.66 seconds and reuses the one passing
metadata query in 0.004 seconds. This does not demonstrate error detection or
neighboring-business-proof reuse for that edit.

A second real edit sets the text generation to zero and is rejected by the
original metadata assertion in **84.74 seconds**. Exact repair compiles in
0.57 seconds and reuses that one query in 0.003 seconds. Both negative models
are independently reconstructed. Keep the first edit's timeout visible: this
is selective error detection, not a demonstrated practical editing workflow.

Keep the cache experimental. Its original resolver remains on misses, so it
does not establish body absence or independence from callee growth. The next
boundary work must separate checked origin/lifetime preservation from the
current-byte frame, using the existing memory/service composition rules. Do not
promote scenario success, exact repair reuse or the one fast valid query into a
public practical-assurance claim. Evidence and limitations:
`practical-assurance/issued-footprint-cache-audit-v1.json` and
[the performance record](performance-and-invalidation.md).

Checked cut transport update (2026-09-11): generated local-view cuts now use
`canonical-service-view-cut-v2`. After the existing complete descriptor check,
an additional required assertion checks the view's access context against the
current runtime owner (or null for an empty view), then substitutes that equal
pointer. Ordinary lifetime checks remain. This adds no trusted runtime contract,
does not modify native accessors or guest heap contents, and retains the v1
reader rules for existing proofs. Python and JQ independently require the new
owner assertion for v2 resumptions. All 66 tests in seven targeted Nix shards
pass without skips, including corrupted-owner, expired-owner, nullable-view,
alias and legacy-reader controls; lint, metadata, format and architecture checks
pass. The interim write-dispatch-only substitution was removed after the cut
change proved sufficient for both stalled lifetime properties.

This is progress in checked coverage, not a completed regional
speedup or practical edit latency. The integrated public
`public-conditional-cleanup-cut-owner-v1` check now finishes incomplete in

A follow-up normalizes the context and runtime pointers inside the outgoing

A more general candidate factors the already-enforced separation between

The existing typed-GOTO source-opacity checker accepts the actual cleanup C and

consumer, not a new frame or runtime-invariant theorem.

A solver comparison now passes the **original full caption assertion in
32.50 seconds** using Z3 on the retained four-contract model, without the
additional image-lifetime premise. This is one diagnostic query, not a complete
regional speedup. The source-opacity and raw SMT output audit is
`practical-assurance/opacity-and-smt-audit-v1.json`. The broader pinned-Z3 diagnostic
(`hybrid-solver-region-v2`) is terminal and incomplete in **510.29 seconds**:
457.16 seconds primary checking and 53.10 seconds nonvacuity. It retains packed
SAT safety and checks application assertions individually with field-sensitive
SMT arrays. All 26 safety groups and 15 application queries pass; text-view
metadata and extent time out. The executor defers the remaining **294 of 311**
authored assertions after those failures. This is not a full regional proof or
a cold speedup: 15 completed inventory/safety queries were reused and 33 queries
were fresh. The full caption assertion passes with the pinned solver in 33.44
seconds. Audit: `practical-assurance/hybrid-solver-region-audit-v2.json`.

The real caption-extent corruption is rejected under this four-contract policy
in 53.49 seconds. Repair compiles the exact original model in 0.56 seconds and
reuses the completed caption result in 0.001 seconds with no new solver query.
The negative model is retained and independently reconstructed. Audit:
`practical-assurance/hybrid-source-edit-audit-v1.json`. These selected-assertion
checks overlap the region run; public component editing and neighboring proof
reuse remain open. Solver selection and scheduling remain diagnostic, not
admitted public proof policy. The alternate SMT array encoding also times out
on both text queries at 120.07 seconds. Symbolic execution finishes in
8.47–8.51 seconds and SSA conversion in 4.98–5.05 seconds; the solver remains
unfinished. Audit: `practical-assurance/hybrid-text-encoding-audit-v1.json`.

Inspection of the real cut identifies the next boundary question: preserve the
incoming dynamic text reference and its terminating byte across the length
service and disjoint scratch writes through existing frame/service composition.
Metadata requires the current resolver's full identity, generation, offset,
extent and permission correspondence. Extent also checks arithmetic, admission,
NUL bounds and the zero byte in both exact and source memory. These facts cannot
be inferred from fixed-image success or assumed as a new allocator outcome.
The first compact candidate replaces only pure namespace lookup with a
deterministic function of its complete mutable namespace projection. Null,
unsupported and unknown-selector cases remain concrete, with the original status
range; the abstract outputs grant no successful lookup, bytes or lifetime. Both
real text queries still time out at 120.05 seconds. This is an unregistered
overapproximation, not a matched concrete input-domain comparison.

A separate exact-helper lemma proves status/output equality and world frames
for arbitrary worlds with equal projected namespace inputs in **7.34 seconds**.
Omitting native generation produces the expected counterexample in **2.01 seconds**.
The existing native-context/projection Nix shard passes all four tests without
skips, including family/expiry/permission mutations and native body absence.
Audit: `practical-assurance/namespace-experiments-audit-v1.json`.

At the real loop cut, checked text-address preservation passes in **18.93 seconds**
and namespace preservation in **20.64 seconds**; snapshot activation also passes.
Substituting those equal fields does not complete the original metadata/extent
assertions. Combining the checked substitutions with the functional lookup
abstraction also leaves both unresolved. No normalization or new contract is
integrated. Audit: `practical-assurance/text-frame-normalization-audit-v1.json`.

The trace identifies a broader dependency than pure namespace lookup: the actual
resolver wrapper revalidates origin registration, public-range admission and
allocation lifetime. Its registration path checks lifetime generation and prior
origin consistency even for an already issued reference. The next candidate
must compose preservation of that issued-reference validity across the allowed
length call and disjoint scratch writes, while retaining terminator-byte checks.
Do not repeat pure-lookup abstraction or promote the passing namespace lemma as
that complete frame. Any new summary must include expiry/release, address reuse,
conflicting origins, private-range changes and byte-write controls, with exact
contract/evidence dependencies. Existing four contracts and full application
obligations remain the baseline; nothing new is admitted yet.

The checked write dispatcher is now integrated behind the explicit trusted
`canonical-view-write-dispatch:1` contract. Its applicability assertion remains
mandatory, and conditional receipts identify the changed compilation policy.
Native compilation retains the ordinary indirect call. All 64 tests in seven
targeted Nix shards pass with no skips, including native/symbolic forwarding,
negative controls and receipt rejection; repository metadata, format-registry,
lint and architecture checks pass. This qualifies the integration tests, not a
complete real-component proof.

The first public caller run rejects the old helper's exact overlay binding
before proof execution because shared accessor generation changed. Recheck that
supplier through the public workflow, then retry `cleanup/sync:allocated` with
the current evidence. Preserve the old rejection and measure this generator
invalidation separately from a local authored-C edit. The helper recheck now
passes in 746.87 seconds, with 71 fresh queries and no reuse. Its application
inputs, required assertions and model bounds are unchanged; compilation takes
0.21 seconds and the mutable-entry check takes 357.20 seconds. Previously
rejected stronger memory/exit-frame claims remain rejected. The public caller
retry with the new supplier receipt terminates incomplete after **1,488.74 seconds**
at a 120-second query budget. All 454 bounds properties and the relation's
nonvacuity witness pass, but repeated pointer-query subdivision isolates an
unresolved runtime receiver lifetime check. Application equivalence has not
completed. No integrated full-region speedup or neighboring-proof reuse is yet
established. See [the integration measurements](performance-and-invalidation.md).

The earlier retained experiment (2026-09-11) separates the generated runtime
receiver's lexical lifetime from guest allocation lifetimes. An unregistered
`lexical-runtime-borrow-experiment:2` checks a nonnull receiver against the bound
source runtime before assuming that owner's liveness. The formerly unresolved
single check passes in 15.38 seconds; the receiver/dispatch guards pass separately
in 40.85 seconds and the first 1,024 pointer properties pass in 19.40 seconds.
This changes only generated harness code and remains non-authorizing. Neither
guest heap contents nor guest object lifetime is assumed. Independent controls
using the actual emitted native issuer and accessor validate in-call owner
lifetime and reject use after return, with unrelated dispatch/context behavior
stubbed. Receiver identity alone does not establish the issuer's scope or
exclude an expired binding. The real-model expired-foreign-owner and null-owner
controls now violate the receiver guard in 87.53 and 87.77 seconds respectively.
A retained diagnostic run of that same region terminates incomplete after
1,565.47 seconds at a lifetime check before write dispatch. Its nonvacuity check
passes, but the assumed write-receiver liveness does not solve the earlier
accessor check. The checked cut substitution above supersedes this candidate;
the liveness contract remains unregistered. These experiments omit auxiliary
frame probes and the other eleven regions. Do not mistake selected checks for
a completed region, supported asynchronous borrowing or a public edit/reuse cycle.

Retained dispatch experiment (2026-09-10): the allocated region's single
`resource_text.array_bounds.1` query times out after 120.25 seconds on the
unchanged model. Verbose output reaches symbolic execution in 0.76 seconds but
does not report its completion or solver entry. A detailed trace expands a
scratch-buffer write through an unrelated `LoadString` function-pointer target;
this is symbolic exploration, not a demonstrated feasible program execution.
The actual source loop cut terminates both alignment branches.

A generated-model prototype checks that the write callback is one of the two
proof-runtime write functions, then calls the selected function directly. It
retains the callback-target assertion before the assumption. The original bounds
