dispatch assertion** then pass in **65.12 seconds**, using the retained prototype
GOTO without compilation (19.32 symbolic execution, 37.22 solver). An injected
wrong callback fails precisely the dispatch assertion in **65.56 seconds**.
Application C, original machine-region sources, cut relations, authority and
runtime-contract selections are unchanged. Only the two generated view adapters
and the generated harness change. No pilot or native-link build was needed.
Evidence and exact source-delta audit: `practical-assurance/allocated-dispatch-audit-v1.json`.

This is a diagnostic prototype, not a public proof or a completed region.
Pointer safety, other safety classes, unwinding/progress, nonvacuity and semantic
equivalence remain required. Before integration, qualify the adapter substitution
through an existing checked-summary/equivalence rule or an explicitly selected
conditional runtime contract, with exact native/proof source binding. A passing
callback-target assertion alone does not justify changing the proof subject.
Keep that assertion mandatory; callbacks outside the specialization need the
ordinary model or a separately justified extension. Then complete the same real
consumer's public check and edit/reuse cycle, rather than start another helper
milestone. Neighboring component-proof reuse remains unestablished.

Rejected trials are retained: constant propagation and depth-first exploration
did not complete within 120 seconds; early restoration rejection and unused
neighbor rejection also timed out. `goto-instrument --constant-propagator`
explicitly warns that it is not sound and must not enter the proof path. The
combined points-to/constant-propagation trial was deliberately stopped on that
warning. These results do not support adopting those optimizations.

Focused public checking checkpoint (2026-09-10): `component check --conditional`
now accepts repeatable `--region OPERATION/OBLIGATION` selections and a

product. It preserves all prepared regions, compiles only selected regions,
and marks the others explicitly deferred without execution evidence. Focused
results always withhold supplier-theorem export, even when all selected checks
pass. Unknown selections reject before proof execution. Completed selected
queries can be reused; deferred regions cannot supply cached evidence.

The real public `cleanup/sync:allocated` check takes 77.09 seconds at the same

All 89 tests in seven targeted Nix shards pass without skips, including focused

Audit correction: the earlier 53.78-second retained antecedent observation is

Local conditional readiness work (2026-09-10): the public checker now distinguishes

All 84 tests in ten targeted Nix shards pass without skips, with lint, metadata,

Compilation accounts for only 9.68 cumulative seconds across 12 models. Safety

The next consumer milestone needs focused checking of an existing region with
a realistic budget and visible remaining coverage, before repeating all 12.
Keep the actual allocation, memory, safety and progress obligations; do not
turn this into another standalone helper-proof milestone or count proof regions
as independently liftable components.

The preceding supplier validation matrix was not all green: the private-frame

Conditional supplier checkpoint (2026-09-10): conditional helper theorems now
compose through the existing source-summary, normal-exit alias, wider-entry,

The public Metapad resource-text helper binds its retained shared-buffer source

The public cleanup attempt stops before compiler/solver execution on its five

The public Metapad helper check now reuses 25 queries and reruns the unresolved

All 40 tests pass through four targeted Nix shards without skips, with lint,

The newer checkpoint above adds prior-query import. Conditional packets are

The generated revision-2 Metapad regional run is terminal and **incomplete**:

The real Metapad reconstruction now obtains all three abstractions from the

The updated executor preserves the old completed Metapad model exactly. Its

runtime abstractions. No speedup over the retained selected revision-1 checks

The operator authorized a comparative experiment using explicit trusted runtime

Use the retained Metapad cleanup operation, its real allocation region and the
same application obligations to compare the rigorous baseline with compact,
named and versioned runtime contracts. Keep application logic, caller premises,
alias/lifetime checks, integer behavior, outcomes and effect order explicit.
Validate the contracts separately against the actual runtime or original binary:
agreement between two consumers of the same trusted model is insufficient.
Include failures, boundary values and deliberate incorrect implementations.

Measure preparation, compiler, model, symbolic execution, solver and evidence
reuse separately, then measure the complete local edit cycle. Reuse retained
inputs before rebuilding pilots. First test whether the proposed boundary helps;
do not require another complete strong helper theorem before making that
comparison. If it helps, carry it into the public edit/check/refine/reuse workflow
with exact contract dependencies and invalidation. If it does not, record the
result and revise the boundary before extending the mechanism.

Strong proofs, proofs conditional on named runtime contracts, bounded checking
and differential tests must remain visibly distinct. A conditional result cannot

remain unresolved. The full real network, independent component bodies,
neighboring-proof reuse, pilot, native-link, export and portability exits remain
required for their respective qualification claims.

The initial retained prototype tests issued-reference reuse across allocation.
It preserves the original resolver on cache misses and checks the proposed reuse
against that original resolver in a separate shadow experiment. This is an

The chronological checkpoints below record earlier sequencing and evidence.

Latest measured result (2026-09-10): the issued-reference-access consumer's

## Objective

Prove a definition or connected definition set written as ordinary Portable-C
behaviorally equivalent to the exact generated Behavioral-C implementation,
without enumerating complete paths or forcing source into a synthetic
`init`/`step`/`finish` API. Carry that proof through exact provider objects and
one strong linked dispatch registry before any candidate can execute.

The detailed proof and activation contract is
[`portable-c-contextual-bisimulation.md`](portable-c-contextual-bisimulation.md).

Implementation now follows
[`independent-portable-lifting-plan.md`](independent-portable-lifting-plan.md):
demonstrate decomposition, local memory contracts and neighboring-proof reuse
before expanding component counts. After the design checkpoint below, validate the
expressiveness and implemented semantics of **hand-defined boundaries**. The
[boundary acceptance cases](independent-portable-lifting-plan.md#hand-defined-boundary-acceptance)
govern prerequisite work: representation, transport, local proof and composition
must each be demonstrated, including rejection cases. Automated boundary
recommendations are deferred. This changes sequencing, not proof authority or
the retained real-network, public-workflow and pilot exits.

Boundary design checkpoint (2026-09-08): at the operator's request, pause further
engine extensions to reason through the system as a whole. The proposed
[component boundary design](component-boundary-design.md) separates semantic
contracts, representation relations, cut transport, checked composition and
evidence reuse. It derives obligations using the actual moving-frame
counterexample and worked alias, loop, callback, lifetime, representation and
invalidation examples. It specifies implementation gates; it does not establish
new proof authority or complete the real-network milestone. In particular,
evaluate a stable invocation scope and independent solver-cache coordinates
before implementing the proposed anchor field. The following checkpoints remain
the implementation/evidence record.

Broader design review (2026-09-08):
[the target-family review](component-boundary-design-review.md) finds substantive
gaps in the first proposal and revises its core rules. Backing storage is distinct
from object/mapping identity; open matching preserves input readiness and causal
choices; pending work retains borrows beyond return/cancellation; exceptional
edges transport unwind/resumption state. Frame locality, joint environment
assumptions, ambient state and bounded-live versus bounded-history reasoning are
explicit. A capability matrix and combined adversarial cases govern wider target
claims. These remain proposed semantics; no engine capability or pilot status is
advanced. The real sequential network stays the next implementation milestone.

Final design pass (2026-09-08):
[composition, interpretation and evolution](component-boundary-design-review.md#7-final-pass-composition-interpretation-and-evolution)
adds conditional pointer/variant interpretation, adapter fault-order obligations,
joint representation coherence, partial-abstraction limits, resize transitions,
absence/closure dependencies and explicit adoption/fallback semantics. It tests
composition laws rather than repeating the previous platform-capability review.
The core design now has an explicit stopping rule: return to the real-network
implementation and reopen decisions for concrete counterexamples. No new engine
rule, target qualification or activation authority is established by this review.

Canonical dialog loop boundaries (2026-09-09): moving the real loop from its

phase specialization have not yet made the full paired proof tractable.

Two bounded resolver experiments are also terminal. Reducing only the nested
range-loop bound from six to four on the unchanged model still times out in the
pointer partition and both witnesses. Copying the selected immutable rule into
a value preserves namespace statuses and outputs in a separate 755-property
comparison (25.454s), but its full regional pointer query and witnesses also
time out. Neither experimental change is adopted into production. These results
do not justify another pilot rebuild or an increased solver timeout.

The exact input, model, command, validation and diagnostic records are indexed
in `build/independent-lifting/real-network/checkpoint-lookahead.json`. That index
also separates experimental resolver changes from production lowering. The next
consumer work must reduce repeated checked view/reference transport while
preserving current lifetime, identity, alias and memory correspondence. Full
local and composed proofs, actual callers, the public edit/refine/reuse and
repaired-recovery workflow, and integration exits remain required.

Earlier full-source scan/tail refinement (2026-09-09): the public cleanup proposal adds
an internal `tail` marker at the shared `0x560d` NUL-exit block. Erasing that new
marker leaves production C byte-for-byte unchanged. The existing plan/closure
engine checks four regions (4 entry, 10 allocated, 9 scan and 14 tail transfers;
regions may share transfers). The scan invariant now retains input bounds,
termination and count correspondence even when scratch is null. Successful
scratch additionally retains its extent/termination relation and zero suffix.
This is an explicit conditional cut contract, not caller admission. Public
source checking passes in 3.845s; material and the complete plan are under
`build/independent-lifting/real-network/public-cleanup-scan-tail-v9/`.

The full ordinary-C scan driver includes every early NUL exit, copying/removal,
and nullable scratch with arbitrary valid heap extents. Its first combined

in that experiment driver. The repaired driver includes the production helper
and its required assertion; body inventory has no undefined `spx_*` functions.
Four origin slots replace eight, with overflow assertions retained. The model
has 171 bodies and 9,266 instructions (885,930 bytes); preparation/model/compiler

The repaired complete-inventory run passes bounds, signed-overflow,
undefined-shift and unwinding partitions. Its pointer partition selects 9,956

All six paired branch witnesses time out independently at 120s. Disabling array
field sensitivity on the same complete pointer inventory also times out at

No complete scan proof or reachability witness is established by these runs.

All these queries are terminal. Keep their retained inputs and errors; do not

`build/independent-lifting/real-network/checkpoint-scan-tail.json`.

Replacing the full zero suffix with only the old output byte being zero fails
both the suffix and next-terminator checks in 59.932s. The concrete next-byte
counterexample takes the copy branch: output advances from 536,870,911 to
536,870,912, and the newly exposed byte can be nonzero. Thus the existing
single-byte invariant cannot support this induction. The prototype installs
the range in current input memory before any heap observation, with input and
scratch as distinct live instances. It never reapplies birth-time zeroing.
This is a tested lowering experiment; public range-fact syntax, matching
predecessor/successor transport and independent receipt rules remain to be
implemented. Evidence is in
`build/independent-lifting/real-network/cleanup-zero-suffix-v1/`.

Previous borrowed-prefix checkpoint (2026-09-08): typed service admission now
accepts an existing registered terminated prefix inside the borrowed view after
realizing its reference. It checks current bytes and the actual runtime reader;
capacity, object identity and lifetime remain separate. Lost registration,
truncated visibility, overwritten termination and unavailable reads are rejected.
Both receipt readers bind the implementation, including for services without a
terminated-offset result contract. Operator diagnostics describe the prefix
requirement. All 56 targeted Nix tests pass with zero skips, plus smoke, lint,
metadata freshness and retired-architecture gates in 29.480s.

The real four-transfer constructor retains identical full C and the same cut
proposal. After 120s conversion timeouts, the 300s full cut-query attempt finds
an allocation-history counterexample in 177.013s. Its source has the live scratch
view; the new failure concerns memory scope: the prologue lowers ESP by 28 bytes,
so an allocation ending just below the entry private window overlaps the private
window recomputed at the cut by 28 bytes. Preserve a consistent private/public
scope across cuts; do not discard those bytes or assume away the overlap.
The separate five string-admission/scratch-descriptor assertions and three
unwind assertions pass on this same model in 146.031s. This proves the selected
constructor transport fix under the retained image-input premises; the complete
cut proof still fails at allocation-history admission. The separate required `EBX`/current-import-slot relation remains unrepresented:
the retained hand-authored `exact_projection` proposal is rejected by the parser.
These are prerequisites for the resumed region and full public split workflow.
Evidence is under `real-network/cleanup-entry-allocation-v4/`; the prior scratch
counterexample below remains retained. No pilot rebuild or native link runs.

Previous hand-defined allocation-split checkpoint (2026-09-08): the full 35-unit
cleanup proposal adds one proof marker immediately after allocation (`0x55d7`),
retaining one ordinary C function. Structural planning and public source check
pass; the latter takes 3.573s. The four-transfer constructor model takes
1.696/0.027/0.253s for preparation/model/compiler. Selected invariant, byte-access,
frame and counter checks plus unwinding pass in 54.014s. **Full cut transport
fails** at `spx-bisimulation-capture:allocated:scratch` in 59.909s. Neither the
selected success nor source compilation establishes a sound split.

The counterexample exposes a contract/adaptor mismatch before scratch creation:
the original accepts a current terminated span within the input object, whereas
the source length adapter requires the final byte of its full-capacity view to
be zero. Its service failure produces a null scratch view while the original
has a live allocation. Do not fix this by assuming a zero in unused object
capacity. Establish an explicit checked terminated span and transport it within
the live object, then preserve the resumed `EBX` relation to the current length
import slot. These are the next hand-defined boundary requirements. Both live
and null cut witnesses are reachable (74.036s and 71.701s); witnesses do not
discharge the failed transport assertion. The earlier 14-transfer entry-to-loop
query timed out during SSA conversion at 120.488s. No pilot rebuild or link ran.
Exact evidence is indexed by `real-network/checkpoint-allocation-split.json`.

The subsequent corrected helper qualification closes that local entry/frame
prerequisite. Exact stack-cache discovery now transports the checked cleanup at

The pinned Nix recheck verifies 578 implementation hashes and binds the corrected

summary consumption; do not repeat this entry investigation as a substitute.

The first composition transport check then exposes an existing replay defect:

Five hand-defined tests pass, including mismatched input memory hidden by an

The current-source binding recheck still accepts the existing local helper

The next hand-defined B3/B5 step implements a conditional body-free shared-view

constants, without adding statements or enumerating bytes.

The actual helper's source/service certificate, relation, wider entry and machine

The next concrete expressiveness gap was framing the helper over the cleanup's
scratch-allocation history. Successful release marks an allocation dead without
removing its recorded identity; the current shared entry requires an empty
allocation world. Consequently this conditional CALL fixture's empty world
cannot establish the actual cleanup predecessor premise. The hand-defined
extension must preserve unrelated contents, identities, generations, liveness
and history across the image-only helper and its service, including negative
overlap/stale-reference cases. Resetting the world or merely weakening the entry
guard would not prove that extension.
The retained `service-buffer/cleanup-shared-allocation-history-v2/` probe makes
this limit concrete at the actual CALL with a disjoint 16-byte scratch object
and stack `0x800000`: allocation, successful release and retained dead identity
assertions pass; only the shared-entry assertion fails (11.726s overall).
This is a conditional counterexample to applicability of the current premise,
not a semantic counterexample to the helper or a full cleanup execution. The
earlier probe left the stack arbitrary and also failed allocation admission;
it remains retained separately and is not this evidence.

The checked leaf extension now adds `image-shared-framed-body-free-v1`. A

The framed consumer rule preserves exact image authority and checks unrelated

The actual v5 supplier also passes both readers' parent-premise audit, with six

The fixture creates scratch lifetime directly; it does not prove the cleanup's

6,555 properties in 119.581s. All twelve original trial inputs remain unchanged;

remaining acceptance work. This step changes retained experiments and docs;
production semantics and the preceding targeted test results are unchanged.

Both actual caller contexts obtain their buffer from the same routine at RVA
`0x5817`. Its checked output-cell, contents, allocation and replacement contract
is the next shared incoming-lifetime dependency; direct inspection alone does
not establish it. The [experiment baseline](baselines/2026-09-07-independent-lifting-experiment.md#shared-incoming-buffer-producer)
records the exact two caller paths and retained evidence. Allocation-failure
access outcomes in the cleanup loop remain a separate required boundary check.

The next retained witness confirms that outcome gap using all 35 original
cleanup transfers and the actual candidate C. With a two-byte input and a failed
allocation, the original faults at the null scratch write; the C returns its
provisional `UINT32_MAX` value after the same three service calls. The explicit
fixture memory callback rejects that write and preserves the input bytes. This
is a checked mismatch in outcome representation, not a production memory-policy
theorem or permission to assume allocation succeeds. Evidence is in
`real-network/cleanup-failure-boundary-v1/`; checked logical failure-to-machine
outcome transport remains required for the full loop.

An independent public edit/reuse attempt now changes the qualified resource
helper to pass and return a local copy of its shared-buffer descriptor. Its
interface, relation, binding, service contract and bisimulation intent are
unchanged. `component check metapad resource-text --source --local-contracts`
against `service-buffer/public-qualified-helper-v8` passes host/PE32 compilation
and the local source theorem: two fresh solver queries take 0.534s. The old and
new certificates have identical checked shared contracts, with different source
and authored-GOTO hashes. The SDK is pinned to the already-qualified v7 SDK to
measure this implementation edit independently of unrelated engine changes.
Full public supplier qualification now passes, with 102 fresh queries and zero
reused queries. Current Python and JQ composition readers accept the edited
supplier and reject all eight altered-premise cases; connected preparation also
rejects its qualification when paired with the old source. Regenerating the
retained conditional allocation/release/helper-call consumer takes 2.220s
preparation and 0.025s model generation: all 13 C/header files are identical,
and neither helper implementation body is present. The comparison is against
`cleanup-origin-capacity-v7`, which used supplier v6. This establishes model
independence, not complete parent qualification or compiled-query cache reuse;
the retained parent has only selected regional checks. Both consuming levels
and the public refine/cut/reuse workflow remain required. Exact receipts, input
hashes, reader rejections and timing records are retained in
`service-buffer/helper-edit-v8-result.json` and its referenced artifacts.

The complementary empty-input allocation-failure witness also passes all 4,493
properties in 2.496s. It returns normally and retains the release call despite
the null allocation. Together with the nonempty v1 witness, this rules out
mapping every null allocation to a fault: checked transport must preserve the
actual failed access and service chronology. The combined symbolic v2 attempt
timed out and remains incomplete. These explicit fixture-memory witnesses do
not establish the production heap policy or either caller's incoming lifetime.

Explicit scalar failure transport is now implemented in the existing result
binding and both machine/logical adapters. The retained cleanup proposal maps
`UINT32_MAX` to a memory fault; no source API or allocation premise changes.
The three focused tests check canonical binding identity, malformed cases and
parameter placement, and all u32 results through generated adapters, including
fault-channel propagation and skipping the normal return epilogue on faults.
Seventeen existing overlay/binding tests also pass locally.
The targeted Nix check passes these 20 tests and all 33 repository-boundary
tests, plus smoke, metadata freshness, lint and retired-architecture gates.
Current Python/JQ readers still admit the previously qualified helper v8 and
reject the eight altered-premise cases and stale source pairing, without new
supplier queries or a rebuild. The first custom Nix expression included local
Python bytecode caches and failed the retired-boundary scan; the accepted run
uses the public Git-filtered flake test attributes.

With the generated result transport, the retained nonempty original/C witness
passes all 4,460 properties in 3.835s; erasing the mapping fails the outcome

in 2.546s and retains its normal return and release call. The larger-stack
nonempty v4 attempt times out at 60s; the successful v5 check uses the original

with the result transport generated by the production renderer. Full production
overlay composition, incoming heap lifetime, both real callers and the public

The subsequent cut investigation confirms that resumed regions reset allocation
history to empty. The engine now requires
`spx-bisimulation-allocation-cut-admission:<sync>` at every outgoing cut; both
Python and JQ require its inventory entry. Matching live allocations and matching
released histories are rejected until incoming lifetime transport is implemented.
Null allocation remains admitted when it creates no history. The cut regression
shows that erasing this guard incorrectly admits both nonempty-history cases.
This closes an admission gap; it does not implement local scratch-view transport
or qualify the cleanup loop. The real five-transfer allocation prefix exercises
the generated cut macro at RVA `0x55dd` under its retained conditional input and
service premises. Its focused allocation-cut query finds the expected violated
premise; full cleanup and actual caller coverage remain open.
The current-source replay takes 1.545s preparation, 0.001s model assembly,
0.313s compilation and 31.061s solver/inventory work. It checks the named cut
premise, not a fresh full-prefix qualification. Evidence is in

tests pass under Nix with zero skips, alongside smoke, lint, metadata freshness
and retired-architecture checks (`real-network/allocation-cut-nix-result.json`).

Incoming allocation-state construction now preserves ordered lifetime metadata
while reading imported live storage from the region's current input memory.
It does not replay birth-time zeroing. Later allocations retain fresh
initialization; released generations, owner/family, native generation and birth

without replacing the history. A real-region counterexample also required the

checked for natively numbered input rows. The four constructor regressions and
related allocation/cut/shared-frame checks pass in the final 70-test Nix run,
with zero skips and smoke/lint/freshness/retired-architecture gates passing.

The actual nine-transfer cleanup loop region now passes 4,906 properties from
an explicit incoming allocation relation. The ordinary C loop body is reused

not full loop induction, complete cut transport or either real caller's proof.
Its input still assumes one live scratch allocation, the recorded image-backed
text view and bounded indices/extent. Outgoing facts must establish each actual
successor's full entry relation before these regions compose.

The combined real prefix/resume attempts remain incomplete at 60s and 180s;

The supporting three-level machine regression now passes using checked composed

This closes a supporting composition experiment, not the real-network milestone.

The real network now has ordinary-C candidates and canonical V5 interfaces for

The following paragraph records the prerequisite at the earlier checkpoint;
the scoped image-memory composition rule above now addresses it.
The immediate composition gap has two parts: the retained mutable caller has a
satisfied paired proof but no `source_summary_contracts`, and memory entry
contracts explicitly reject suppliers that themselves consume components.
Implement checked dependency use in source summaries together with transitive
entry/frame composition; removing the rejection alone is insufficient. Require
body absence in both consuming levels and a leaf edit that reuses both levels'
queries while rebinding current supplier evidence. The small regression chain
supports this work; the real network remains the acceptance case.

The preceding implementation adds separate checked mutable cut/exit machine

The path from this unfinished migration to a generally useful operator workflow
is planned in
[`interactive-portable-lifting-roadmap.md`](interactive-portable-lifting-roadmap.md).
It preserves the required pilot and repository exits below, then adds assisted
source authoring, reusable platform coverage, standalone source export, and a
frozen unfamiliar-application trial. Authored acyclic cutpoints now use the checked region engine. Automatic
cutpoint proposals remain implementation work. The first body-free summary
strategy admits checked pure scalar operations; general stateful composition
and its real-caller scaling exits remain unfinished.

- [x] Add strict proof-intent, proof-plan, exact-C-slice, proof-world, and
  contextual-refinement formats with registry ownership.
- [x] Make source proof annotations compile away in production and become real
  forced entry/sync barriers in proof builds.
- [x] Generate one exact entry obligation plus authored acyclic or cyclic sync
  obligations, checking that every exact cycle is cut; specialize its exact-C and
  source barrier slices, derive local bounds, and materialize zero paths.
- [x] Compile one GOTO model per obligation, prove authored relational
  assertions with formula slicing, and check source-language definedness through
  complete property-class and loop inventories partitioned over the paired
  exact/source obligation entry using an explicitly pinned SAT backend.
- [x] Bind typed services, shared memory, state/parameter captures, connected
  provider summaries, proof plans, exact slices, and contextual receipts into
  direct Portable-C provider qualification.
- [x] Migrate the GNU Hello string pilots, jq output-value pipeline, and DX-Ball
  DirectDraw initialization tail to the unified proof input.
- [x] Generate the sole strong module dispatch registry from the exact selected
  portable overlays; require unique strong implementation symbols and emit a
  selection/payload/link-map-bound activation receipt.
- [x] Make `native-realization-v2` and every SDK/test/deployment execution gate
  reject missing, stale, weak, duplicate, partial, or source-only dispatch
  authority.
- [x] Reject unmapped compatibility exact-C, implicit/null proof intents,
  explicit source `goto` cycles, invalid typed views, and receipt-free aggregate
  execution as non-authorizing inputs.
- [ ] Discharge every real pilot shard, retaining stable fail-closed diagnostics
  for any genuine mismatch or bounded solver limit.
- [ ] Pass focused unit/constructor tests, refresh generated registries, and pass
  the applicable Hello, jq, DX-Ball, native PE32, metadata, and repository gates.

## Current findings

Nullable external-service byte views now have a shared production/proof decoder.
The admitted result uses the checked EAX origin remainder and a fixed or unsigned
scalar-argument extent. Allocation failure returns a canonical empty view.
Non-null results carry the complete reference and use the enclosing runtime for
access; every read/write resolves the live generation, permissions and bounds.
No accessor context points into the returning service's stack. The public
`spx_view_write_u8` helper complements the existing read helper. Both receipt
readers require the shared decoder in the consuming adapter's implementation
closure. Nullable operation parameters/results, state, nested records and callback
types remain closed pending their own admission and reconstruction rules.

The normal allocation/write/read/release fixture passes in 702.26 seconds at the
existing 300-second per-query production ceiling, and both full receipt readers
accept it. Its 30- and 60-second diagnostic runs remain recorded as timeouts.
This proves the fixture's admitted behavior, not interactive performance or a real
application provider. Host and PE32 compilation plus logical-type/reader checks
pass. Focused access fixtures now use their actual one-write transcript bound;
separate read, write and bounds cases pass in 20.92 seconds total with the original
30-second query ceiling. The complete operation retains all accesses together.
The byte mutation is rejected by the normal checker in 332.83 seconds. A retained
30-second query also rejects it at the release call's public-memory boundary in
27.52 seconds, before retirement can hide the changed byte. All 26 focused tests
pass in 210.95 seconds with zero skips. Initial affected validation found one
stale test expectation for the unused-helper reference; that expectation now
requires the unevaluated `sizeof` form.

The next affected run hit a 30-second timeout in the existing reference-selector
renaming shard. Its exact Nix derivation passes in isolation: five tests in
25.64 seconds. The host allows sixteen concurrent Nix builds, while a normal
proof shard can launch four solver queries. The retry with two local builds passes
in 1,237.71 seconds with unchanged proof/test inputs and unchanged deadlines.
The returned-view Nix shard passes all thirteen tests in 1,101.61 seconds. The public
test runner also accepts `--max-jobs` and opt-in `--keep-going`, so integration
checks can avoid solver oversubscription and retain independent results after
another shard fails. All eleven runner tests pass, including failure propagation,
the smoke gate and option preservation through cached evaluation. The subsequent
combined validation failed after 263.37 seconds with a Nix realization-registration
conflict; its 24 code/test inputs were unchanged. Evidence is under
`build/validation-scheduling/`.

That validation also exposed avoidable dependency growth. The fixture package
initializer imported scaffolding and its complete production module index, while

directory reference. Developer-tool consumers now import their owning modules;
the fixture facade remains lightweight. Catalog dependency discovery uses the
existing strict catalog parser and rejects malformed extensions. The buffer shard
falls from 583 file inputs to 339, excluding the runner, production-wide index
and whole `nix/` directory. All 41 focused tooling tests pass, including independent
tool edits, actual fixture edits, ordinary JSON includes and malformed catalogs.
The shard report included measured peak RSS in its content-addressed output,
making identical test runs capable of producing different output identities.
The measurement now goes to the build log; correctness and inventory remain in
the deterministic report. Two regression cases check identical reports under
different measurements and preservation of test failures. All six Nix test-gate
tests pass. The real native shard passes seven tests with zero skips, and Nix's
`--rebuild` check on its resolved derivation succeeds with an identical report.
The combined retry through `--max-jobs 2 --keep-going` passes in 1,498.38 seconds;
the returned-view shard passes thirteen tests in 1,096.28 seconds with zero skips.
Its original snapshot predates the coverage fixes below: `runner.py` and its test
changed while the run was active. No proof assumptions or receipts were weakened.

The deterministic-report retry exposed another validation gap before finishing:

used `context.pythonEnv`, omitting the transfer-native extension present in the

Five Bochs integration tests lacked their backend fixture, and three protocol

1,357.45 seconds with unchanged recorded inputs. Evidence is under

The preceding batch connected local calls to runtime references:

instance of the consulted class. Manually seeded objects and unrelated call IDs
remain rejected. Call-prestate snapshots and final allocation equality preserve
this evidence; release tombstones retain it while the native resolver checks live
generations, full metadata, bounds and permissions.

and complete ordered range table; every lifetime call also needs matching native
site evidence and runtime authorization. Unrelated native object, producer and
family insertions may change numeric selectors without changing a requirement.
The content-bound `portable-allocation-correspondence.json` output is diagnostic;
the existing strong dispatch/link path remains the execution authority.

update or original executable run was used.

The preceding batch implemented paired allocation/release transitions:

The existing paired call oracle now applies owned allocation and whole-instance
release transitions when supplied native inventories establish the local producer
correspondence. Every call site's effect, selected contract identity and physical
argument count must agree with its native range rule. Validation rows are not
mistaken for producer rows. Normal adapter/provider admission still rejects these
effects without the required canonical/caller integration; both strong receipt
readers retain their existing lifetime gate.

Raw calls apply effects after retaining their input-memory/lifetime snapshot.
Typed calls commit once before exposing a result (or at finish when unused), so
an allocation is live before result-reference resolution. Allocation instances
retain zero/uninitialized byte semantics, actual sizes, owner/family and fresh
native generations; successful release retires the instance and failed release
preserves it. The fresh-result domain uses the checked native namespace's
representable one-past limit. Caller flag domains, requested-size bounds and
model capacity remain assertions before response assumptions. A retained
counterexample exposed the prior mismatch at base `0xfffffff0`, size 16.

The 51-test focused group passes in 72.77 seconds (80.72 with preparation).
Solver cases cover real GlobalAlloc/GlobalFree profiles through raw and typed
record/replay, repeated result reads, smaller reuse, null allocation, failed
release, uninitialized bytes, fixed/scaled/product size policies, capacity
exhaustion, source argument substitution and removed/doubled transitions.
Positive cases include reachability witnesses. The size-policy variants are
explicit synthetic profile fixtures. Evidence is under
`build/reference-namespace/allocation-calls/`.
The initial affected validation passes in 260.33 seconds. A subsequent frame
audit explicitly limits the transition path to direct CALL frames; native tail
offsets and indirect-target correspondence remain unsupported. All eight final
allocation-call tests pass in 42.51 seconds, including ignored release results
that commit at typed finish. Final affected validation first stopped on HTTP 502
errors from the remote Determinate build-trace service, not a test assertion.
Retrying the same unchanged inputs with substitution disabled and local builders
passes in 163.17 seconds. The per-run Nix overrides and both terminal results are
retained; host Nix configuration was not changed. Metadata and final whitespace
checks pass, and the installed trial/SDK comparison remains unchanged.

A retained mutable-buffer model allocates, writes and releases through the real
GlobalAlloc/GlobalFree profile-derived oracle and typed source calls. Its proof
and reachability witness pass. Changing only the source's written byte is rejected
at the paired call prestate before release, even though both final instances are
retired. The first reported assertion is the combined lifetime typed-call match,
not the more specific memory assertion expected by the diagnostic script; the
expectation was corrected against the retained terminal outputs without rerunning
the solver. These models remain supplied-inventory diagnostics, not a qualified
component. All validation processes are terminal.

This implements the call transition inside the shared engine, not incoming
caller ownership or an authoritative normal-provider inventory. Runtime reference
admission, caller/frame bindings, cross-cut instance reconstruction and bounds,
and normal call-outcome, typed-adapter/receipt integration remain required before the
Metapad component can qualify. No pilot rebuild or trial SDK update was used.

The preceding batch implemented local producer preparation:

Local allocation preparation now derives direct producer sites from the replayed
transfer plan, canonical object authority and parsed resolved environment. Native
planning and this preparation share the import-frame constructor and external-tail
classification. The report binds exact transfer/instruction identities, selected
contract behavior and physical call frames. Cross-module inputs fail before
preparation; unrelated environment incompleteness stays visible without requiring
a complete linked module. The normal provider emits `allocation-producer-inputs.json`
after its image/object binding checks and includes its digest in qualification
inputs. It remains non-authorizing and does not supply numeric native selectors.

The focused 56-test group passes in 0.89 seconds (8.23 with preparation), covering
native call/tail frame equality, repeated producers, stale and mismatched inputs,
missing/ambiguous/nonallocator contracts, callback provenance, indirect coverage,
and provider preparation before component binding or compilation. A retained
Metapad replay scans 5,521 transfers in 1.91 seconds. Its installed environment
correctly lacks the current owned-result contract; a separately labelled current
profile diagnostic finds 23 direct GlobalAlloc sites, including `0x55d1`, `0x5838`
and `0x59aa`. Both reports retain 633 unresolved indirect sites. The authored
diagnostic authority and substituted profile are not installed trial inputs.
Evidence is under `build/reference-namespace/producer-inputs/`.
Affected validation passes in 164.64 seconds with unchanged validated inputs,
including metadata, production lint, architecture boundaries and the selected
pure/solver shards. Final whitespace and installed-trial comparisons pass; all
processes are terminal.

Complete producer coverage, incoming caller ownership, live instances and checked
allocation/release transitions across cuts remain unfinished. Runtime-locator and
allocator-service admission remain closed. No pilot build, SDK repin or original
PE execution was needed for this preparation check.

The preceding batch checked supplied selector correspondence:

Native rendering and proof allocation correspondence now share producer grouping,
object-selector validation and complete-inventory family numbering in
`external/range_bindings.py`. The proof context no longer uses its local authority
ordinal as a native producer selector. It checks the supplied native authority's
full class/extent/policy/locator against the canonical proof authority, retains
the exact contract identity, and translates native producer/family numbers into
local allocation records. Projected native references retain the actual native
producer selector. Missing inventory cannot create a correspondence.

The shifted-inventory fixture uses component authority/family index 1, native
object/family index 2, and two native producer sites at selectors 2 and 4 sharing
representative 2. Actual generated native allocation/release helpers agree with
the projected full reference tuple and preserve generation expiry on smaller
address reuse. Local-family and local-selector substitutions are rejected;
positive paths have coverage witnesses. The initial 28-test compatibility group
passes, and the 21-test repaired projection/ownership group passes in 30.66 seconds.
The repair removed a fixture assumption that release-site index 2 always meant
the CRT family and supplied the required coverage function inventory.
Evidence is under `build/reference-namespace/producer-selectors/`.
The broader 69-test group passed 68 cases, including the normal two-region native
image proof, and exposed the GlobalFree fixture's old owner-slot pointer encoding.
That wrapper now passes the pointer through the common release helper's pointer
argument. Both GlobalAlloc/GlobalFree admission tests pass after the repair in
0.66 seconds (8.58 with preparation); production code did not change for it.
Affected validation passes in 257.40 seconds with unchanged validated and
supporting inputs. Metadata is current, the final whitespace check passes and
all processes are terminal. The installed trial and SDK remain unchanged.

This checks correspondence within the supplied native inventories. The normal
provider still needs to reconstruct and bind their origin/completeness from its
canonical inputs, establish incoming caller instances, and carry allocation/release
transitions and lifetimes across cuts with checked bounds. Normal runtime-locator
and allocator-service admission remain closed, including after a valid supplied
mapping and local instance binding. No pilot rebuild, SDK repin or original PE
execution is part of this batch.

The preceding batch unified contract identities:

Native external sites and Portable-C service overlays now obtain their profile
behavior from the same `external/resolved_contract.py` normalization. The checked
site extends that behavior with its physical frame and callback evidence. Native
range rules retain their established identity digest; the proof adapter now
carries that same digest into each call specification and behavior identity.
The digest binds the import, selected profile entry, ABI, arity, disposition and
normalized effects, including allocation family, initialization and release.
It deliberately excludes site addresses and argument-expression nodes.

The focused 116-test group passes in 20.24 seconds (28.77 with preparation),
covering real GlobalAlloc/GlobalFree profile-to-native-rule correspondence,
physical call/tail-frame separation, profile-only changes through the production
overlay and proof transcript, adapter replay, callback/variadic compatibility,
and allocation guards. Both reader checks accept a valid generated adapter and
reject six rehashed missing/changed identity or missing-normalizer mutations.
Evidence is under `build/reference-namespace/producer-identities/`.
Affected validation passes in 340.72 seconds with unchanged final validated and
supporting inputs. The first attempt found one unused import left by the hashing
refactor; it was removed and metadata refreshed before the successful run.
All processes are terminal. Final whitespace and installed-trial comparisons pass.

This removes independently normalized contract identities; it does not establish
the linked producer-site selector or native family-number mapping. Complete
incoming instance inventory, caller ownership, allocation/release transitions,
cross-cut lifetime state and instance-loop bounds remain unfinished. Runtime
locator and allocator-service admission remain closed. No pilot rebuild or trial
SDK repin is part of this batch.

The preceding batch implemented allocation-instance projection:

The existing proof allocation records now carry a bound authority-rule selector
and a native generation separately from their internal birth token. Binding
requires an already-live allocation with matching full base/size, allocator family,
owner and native object class. Only the native allocation kind/lifetime policy is
eligible. A repeated identical binding is idempotent; changed bindings and reuse
of any recorded native generation, including a retired one, are rejected.

The normal reference adapter now obtains its namespace context through a bounded
projection of these records. The projection exports only live bound instances,
retains their actual allocation sizes and native generations, and uses the same
native resolver for identity, extent and interior-pointer checks. Its rule
ordinals are local context selectors, not claims about linked native producer-site
indexes. Call-time allocation snapshots and final lifetime equality include the
new correspondence fields. Partially populated instance metadata is rejected.

Generated native allocation/release bodies and the production proof world agree
on full reference tuples, including a native epoch of 101 versus proof birth
token 2, native table compaction, and smaller address reuse. Removed-generation,
expired-instance and omitted-snapshot-field mutations are rejected. The initial
48-test compatibility group passes in 121.82 seconds (129.86 with preparation),
including the normal two-region image-view checker. A subsequent 15-test policy
group passes in 29.32 seconds; the final focused policy case additionally checks
fixed-rule lifetime mismatches and passes in 0.76 seconds. Evidence is under
`build/reference-namespace/allocation-projection/`.

Affected validation passes in 244.40 seconds with unchanged final validated and
supporting inputs. Metadata is refreshed, the final whitespace check passes,
and all validation processes are terminal. No pilot rebuild or SDK repin was
part of this batch.

This implements instance projection, not producer or caller qualification. The
normal runtime-locator guard still fails even after a local binding is supplied,
and allocator/release services remain blocked. A complete incoming instance
inventory, checked producer/family-selector correspondence, call effects, caller
lifetimes, cross-cut allocation state and per-obligation instance-loop bounds
are still required before opening that gate. The trial and SDK pin
remain unchanged.

The preceding batch repaired allocation freshness:

Allocation freshness now excludes the complete checked mapped image in the proof
world, before any image reference is enrolled. Native owned-range registration
rejects overlap with the mapped image and every registered live external range,
including borrowed ranges. Image collisions report native diagnostic `0x2113`;
live-range collisions retain `0x2111`. Rejected registrations preserve the range
inventory, ownership and generation sequence. Adjacent allocations, null results,
and reuse after a successful release remain supported.

Two retained pre-fix solver cases reproduced allocation into an unborrowed image
and native allocation over a borrowed range. The 48-test focused group passes in
25.75 seconds (33.65 with preparation), including symbolic extents, image-boundary
crossings, zero-size lifetimes, identity preservation, three removed-check
mutations, host execution and PE32 compilation. Evidence is under
`build/reference-namespace/allocation-freshness/`. The normal checker still blocks
allocator services and unresolved runtime instances; these checks do not supply
caller ownership or admit those services.

Affected validation passes in 237.64 seconds with unchanged validated and
supporting inputs, refreshed metadata, and a passing final whitespace check.
All validation processes are terminal. No pilot rebuild or trial SDK repin was
needed for this batch.

The retained Metapad caller trace is under
`build/metapad-trial/caller-ownership/`. The count word is reserved and initialized
inside the guest function beginning at RVA `0x56b5`; native-ingress captured
argument rules cannot supply its local-frame authority. The text producer at
`0x5817` writes an allocation result through its EDI out pointer, initially using
the `GetFileSize` result plus two at `0x5838`; a conversion path can replace it
with the allocation at `0x59aa`. The null-result branch calls `0x3543`, whose body
has a return path, then continues toward the buffer use. It is not a checked
non-returning failure contract. Caller qualification must preserve allocation
failure, size arithmetic, out-pointer aliasing and intervening service effects.
These are static observations, not a complete caller or lifetime proof. The
installed trial and SDK remain unchanged, and no original PE was executed.

The preceding batch added explicit variable-size allocation extents:

Variable-size allocation authority now has an explicit `extent_mode:
instance_remainder` in the existing object-rule format. It is admitted only for
allocation/resource locators with the corresponding lifetime. The rule's positive
`extent` is the required minimum; the canonical reference extent is the checked
live range size minus its locator offset. Omitting the mode preserves fixed-size
semantics and existing fixed-rule payloads/hashes. A base address alone cannot
realize a dynamic extent in the Python diagnostic API.

The native plan carries the mode into its generated table, and the shared
native/proof resolver compares offsets, requested access, one-past pointers and
reference metadata against the selected instance's extent. Distinct live
allocations of one class can have different sizes. Release and address reuse
preserve generation expiry even when the replacement allocation is smaller.
Missing producer bindings still block the native plan, and the normal proof
harness still rejects unqualified runtime locators, including the new mode.

This removes a representational prerequisite for variable-size text buffers;
it does not supply incoming caller ownership or authorize allocator calls.
The retained Metapad disassembly shows a runtime-derived allocation size at
RVA `0x55d1` (increment the preceding call's EAX result, then pass it with flags
`0x40`). That is static evidence of the requirement, not a caller-lifetime proof.
Evidence and validation are under `build/reference-namespace/dynamic-extents/`.
The installed trial, SDK pin and pilot build policy remain unchanged.

The focused 51-test group passes in 79.95 seconds including preparation. All nine
final dynamic-extent tests pass in 3.44 seconds, including host execution and
PE32 compilation of the generated native helpers. Affected validation passes in
281.20 seconds with unchanged validated inputs. Final whitespace and installed
trial comparisons pass. These results validate dynamic extent handling; incoming
runtime-instance admission and allocator call qualification remain incomplete.

The preceding batch repaired native image-view cut reconstruction:

Native view cuts now reconstruct the full reference tuple by resolving the
reached machine address through the production operation's bound authority
selector and access permissions. The generated overlay retains its checked
selector map for this handoff. A source reference or an authored cut projection
cannot choose a different selector. Full native object extent and interior offset
are preserved; the visible extent follows the declared fixed/value extent or the
native remaining span. NUL termination is a separate relation within that span,
not a definition of the native object's size. Shared input admission exposes the
full canonical origin, including its prefix before an interior pointer.

The normal checker uses this decoder for incoming assumptions and outgoing
metadata assertions. Method, access-context, full-origin memory, and continuation
checks remain required. Both strong receipt readers now require
`native_cut_reference_decoding`; predecessor receipts are insufficient.
A register-only resumed decoder no longer shifts ESP into an unnecessary
synthetic frame. The full two-region fixture initially reproduced a false
continuation-state mismatch from that shift; preserving ESP repairs it without
weakening the continuation equality assertion. Stack-decoding cases retain their
synthetic frame and still require a complete continuation relation.

Generated-overlay/barrier solver cases accept full native extents and legitimate
interior-pointer movement, reject altered identities, generations, permissions,
extents and overlapping-namespace substitution, and reproduce the old flat
extent rejection on a valid view. NUL bounds and positive-path coverage are
checked. The normal contextual checker also passes a two-region image-view
fixture. Evidence and validation for this batch are under
`build/reference-namespace/native-cuts/`.

The six native-view tests pass, including the normal checker and positive-path
coverage. The retained overlapping-authority two-region proof completes in 47.04
seconds; both readers accept it and reject missing/false cut-policy, missing
inventory, wrong-world and missing-input mutations. The 39-test compatibility
run exposed two subcase failures in an already-incomplete receipt fixture; those
policy mutations now use the fully validated normal-engine receipt, and the
repaired two-test receipt group passes. Affected validation passes in 247.40
seconds with unchanged validated inputs and current metadata. Final whitespace
and installed-trial comparisons pass.

These checks validate cut reconstruction for admitted image instances. Incoming native
stack/heap/TLS/imported-data/resource instances, caller ownership and cross-cut
allocation lifetimes remain unfinished. Allocator calls remain blocked; the
Metapad inputs and SDK pin remain unchanged. No full pilot rebuild is part of
this batch.

The preceding authority-inventory batch established the native runtime handoff:

The normal provider now passes its parsed `MachineObjectAuthorityV2` inventory
through the contextual checker to the existing proof world. Native and proof
resolution share the C implementation in `transfer/reference_namespace.py`;
extracting that implementation leaves generated native C byte-identical.
The proof runtime realizes image-relative rules against the checked image and
preserves the native identity, generation, canonical extent and full permission
metadata. It applies native selector/ambiguity and interior/one-past checks.
A valid native tuple can be validated and enrolled during realization without a
preceding resolve call. The origin recorder now preserves full runtime permission
bits, including an image authority's execute bit, while realization still checks
the requested access.

Each obligation binds the canonical authority core as a proof input whose hash
is the authority identity. The aggregate receipt binds the same inventory to its
proof world, and both readers require `native_reference_authority_bound` plus
the per-model authority input. Earlier receipts are insufficient. Namespace
helper loop limits are derived from the bound rule count and selector byte
lengths; source and exact-region unwind limits remain unchanged. The Python
evidence reader independently reconstructs those command arguments.

The 53-test focused group passes in 43.74 seconds (45.33 with preparation).
A fresh normal-engine fixture uses the native namespace through the generated
runtime and passes in 2.10 seconds. Both readers accept it and reject five
missing/false-policy, missing-inventory, wrong-world and missing-input cases.
The full checker also produces an explicit counterexample for an unqualified
runtime locator. Evidence is under `build/reference-namespace/authority-inputs/`.
Affected validation passes in 236.24 seconds with unchanged validated inputs
and refreshed metadata. The final whitespace check passes and the installed
Metapad file comparison is unchanged.

This connects the authority inventory and image realization; it does not provide
qualified TLS, imported-data, captured-stack, allocation or resource instances.
Consulting those locators triggers the required
`spx-bisimulation-reference-locator-qualified` assertion rather than using the
flat namespace or assuming the object is absent. The flat resolver remains only
for direct diagnostics without an authority input; those results cannot satisfy
the new strong receipt policy. Native extent/generation reconstruction at cuts,
incoming caller ownership and allocation/lifetime composition remain required.
No full pilot rebuild or Metapad SDK repin is part of this batch.

The preceding origin-record batch separated physical storage from identity:

The issued-reference table now separates logical domain/object/generation from
physical object base and the proof world's allocation lifetime token. Both the
existing flat resolver and the mapped realizer use this table. Recording an
interior native reference normalizes its physical base; a logical identity cannot
move to another base or be rebound to a later lifetime. Borrowed-span allocation
exclusion uses physical storage and the internal lifetime token, independently
of the native generation. Repeated enrollment of an identical origin, including
different interior offsets, does not consume additional capacity.

Nine new tests execute the actual native resolver and allocation helpers with
the production proof world. They cover full-width IDs, different instances of
one native object class, release/address reuse, borrowed storage, conflicting
mappings, repeated resolution, forged metadata, symbolic addresses/offsets,
full-origin private-range exclusion and capacity exhaustion. Four
removed-check/arithmetic mutants reproduce failures;
successful paths require coverage witnesses. The initial 64-test focused group passes
in 56.13 seconds (57.54 with preparation). Evidence is under
`build/reference-namespace/origin-records/`. The initial capacity fixture also
asserted success at the overflowing insertion; removing that competing assertion
lets it check the required capacity diagnostic directly.
Affected validation then exposed an existing code-generation expectation that
the private-range check was inline in the resolver. It now follows the shared
recorder; a solver regression separately checks the private prefix of an origin
whose visible pointer is public. The 21-test repair group passes in 18.50 seconds.
Final affected validation passes in 146.19 seconds with unchanged validated and
supporting inputs, current metadata and a passing whitespace check. The installed
Metapad file comparison remains unchanged.

This is storage/lifetime separation, not native namespace admission. The default
resolver still constructs address-valued object IDs, and the enrollment helper
does not carry authority-selector or interior-pointer rules. Native origin
selection, canonical extents, incoming ownership and cross-cut lifetime
composition remain required. Allocator calls remain blocked. The normal proof
input must bind the native authority inventory before mapped origins can qualify
general native-reference behavior; no pilot rebuild or trial SDK repin is needed
for this primitive batch.

Four retained solver diagnostics make that admission gap concrete. Even after
enrolling native metadata, the default resolver returns its flat tuple and
ignores an unknown authority selector. A native authority with interior pointers
disabled rejects a changed offset that the mapped proof realizer currently
accepts. Conversely, a valid native tuple can realize without an earlier resolve,
whereas the proof table requires enrollment. Each correspondence assertion has an
explicit counterexample in under 0.3 seconds; these are unresolved discrepancies,
not qualification evidence. The next integration must retain the selected
authority rules and their live instances, including selector ambiguity and
interior policy, and justify initial reference availability. Merely importing
successful native reference tuples is insufficient. See
`build/reference-namespace/origin-records/admission-audit/result.json`.

Cutpoint and connected-memory consumers now realize references through the
runtime ABI instead of computing addresses from object IDs. A shared proof-only
helper checks the visible span, full object bounds and realization status without
issuing an origin. Source byte expressions check their visible index before
forming an address. Cut context reconstruction includes successful realization;
full-reference memory comparison starts at the realized address minus the
reference's checked interior offset, including bytes before the visible view.

Connected wrappers save each input's realized address at the exact call and
check the source call's realization before replay. Equal logical metadata cannot
hide different physical mappings or an expired source origin. Native-resolver
fixtures reproduce false acceptance when the address comparison is removed,
and when full-object comparison incorrectly starts at the interior pointer.
Canonical null references and remaining-span observations are covered too.
The 67-test focused group passes in 57.25 seconds (65.63 with preparation);
evidence is under `build/reference-namespace/cut-consumers/`.

These changes remove consumer address arithmetic. They do not generalize the
flat proof resolver, its canonical requested/reference/NUL extents, or the
generation/offset reconstruction policy at cuts. Incoming ownership and
cross-cut lifetime composition remain required, allocator calls remain blocked,
and no pilot rebuild or external-trial SDK update is part of this batch.

Typed service adapters now consume the runtime's realized address rather than
requiring a native object ID to equal that address. Borrowed-interior results
derive their offset from the checked input address, preserve the origin's
authority metadata and must realize back to the physical service result.
Proof and native result lowering also retain full-width domain/object/generation
fields instead of truncating them to 32 bits. The adapter renderer receipt now
includes the borrowed-input implementation; Python and the strong jq reader
reject the predecessor inventory without that dependency.

Small solver fixtures execute generated service adapters with the production
native resolver, using 64-bit domain/object IDs and nonzero interior offsets.
They cover nullable results, expired/forged origins, native result lowering,
and mutations restoring address arithmetic, truncated IDs or altered authority
permissions. Positive result paths require a coverage witness; the old interior
arithmetic cannot hide behind an unsatisfiable result constraint. Evidence and
validation records are under `build/reference-namespace/typed-services/`.
This repairs service consumers, not the general proof/native namespace theorem;
the subsequent consumer work is recorded above. Canonical extents, caller
ownership and cross-cut lifetime composition remain integration work.
Allocator calls remain blocked; no pilot rebuild or Metapad SDK repin is needed
for this batch.

The allocator-admission trace found a concrete native-reference issue in Hello's
ASCII comparison source. Its fast path compared only `base.object`, although the
native resolver supplies an object ID plus generation and interior offset.
The production resolver creates valid equal-ID references for different slices
of one allocation, and for distinct live instances of one object class. The old
source returned equality without reading their different bytes. It now uses the
existing `spx_ref_difference` helper and takes the shortcut only at zero distance.

Four small CBMC tests compile the actual authored source with its generated
interface headers, shared reference helpers and production native resolver.
They cover shared-allocation offsets, distinct allocation generations, the
unchanged same-location shortcut and a mutant restoring the object-only test.
The old source fails the shared-allocation regression; all four final tests pass.
The 15-test focused compatibility group passes in 5.66 seconds (7.04 seconds
including preparation). Affected validation passes in 79.93 seconds with unchanged
validated inputs and refreshed metadata. Evidence is under
`build/reference-namespace/ascii-compare/`.

This is a source repair, not a new pilot qualification. The flat proof resolver
still uses address-valued object identities. A general correspondence with the
native object/generation/offset namespace remains an explicit prerequisite for
allocator-call and cross-cut lifetime admission. The allocator blocker remains;
Metapad's installed source, intents and SDK pin are untouched. The required Hello
qualification is deferred to the integration checkpoint, not waived.

Shared call replay now checks allocation lifetimes at the call as well as public
bytes. The call record retains allocation count and one universally checked
allocation-index witness, keeping snapshot storage constant per call. Machine
and typed replay compare the saved instance's semantic fields, including live
state and generation; typed exact recording saves the same observation. A
premature source release is rejected even when zero-filled bytes and final
allocation inventories agree. Removing the new check reproduces acceptance.
The final exact inventory is deliberately not used as the earlier observation.

Six new tests execute ten small CBMC queries across machine and typed call paths.
The 60-test focused group passes in 50.25 seconds (58.26 seconds with preparation).
A fresh normal-engine proof takes 1.76 seconds; Python and jq both accept it and
reject rehashed missing/false `call_allocation_lifetimes_checked` policies.
Affected validation passes in 218.56 seconds with unchanged validated inputs
and refreshed metadata. Evidence is under
`build/metapad-trial/call-allocation-lifetimes/`. Historical
receipts without this policy are superseded for current authority. This is a
prerequisite for allocator-call integration, not admission of those calls:
`proof_service_lifetime_effect_unsupported`, caller-owned initial ranges and
cross-cut lifetime composition remain unresolved. No pilot is rebuilt for this
shared-world batch.

Fixed GlobalAlloc/GlobalFree now have checked native range contracts. GlobalAlloc
moved from opaque native callthrough into the kernel32 range profile with requested
extent, nullable result, `kernel32.global-fixed` ownership, admitted flag mask 64
and explicit flag-controlled zero initialization. Generic allocation metadata is
validated at profile loading, checked receipt parsing and native lowering. The
native pre-call path rejects unsupported flag bits before host execution;
GlobalFree releases argument zero only on an `eax_zero` outcome and requires the
same allocation family. Failed release preserves the instance. The retained
Metapad transformer call at RVA `0x55d1` supplies flag `0x40`, within this domain.
Movable objects, reallocation and caller-owned initial buffers remain unsupported.

Six new tests include three small CBMC queries and a removed-flag-guard mutant.
The 83-test focused group passes in 7.68 seconds (15.15 seconds with preparation),
and the complete generated runtime compiles for IA-32 in 0.11 seconds without
linking or execution. Evidence is under
`build/metapad-trial/global-allocation-contracts/`. Final affected validation passes
in 139.48 seconds with unchanged validated inputs and refreshed metadata. The
first affected run exposed the stale callthrough-profile inventory expectation;
its repaired nine-test group also passes. The initialization field is a
selected API contract, not a claim that native lowering initializes memory.
Contextual proof-call admission still rejects both APIs until the contract is
connected to the paired proof world and checked caller lifetimes. The external
trial remains pinned and no pilot rebuild is required for this contract batch.

The paired sparse proof world now implements bounded allocation births,
family/owner-checked release and per-instance logical generations. Address reuse
expires old aliases and resets the visible write/shadow history; fresh memory is
either zero initialized or drawn from a shared per-instance byte function.
Zero-size instances grant no byte access. Full-span access checks reject crossing
into untracked bytes or an adjacent allocation, and issued one-past references
retain their original owner. Paired memory checks compare lifetime state while
allowing different write widths and history positions. Seven new solver tests
include two removed-guard mutants; the boundary regression failed before its
repair. The 52-test focused group passes in 42.33 seconds (51.25 seconds including
preparation). Evidence is under
`build/metapad-trial/proof-allocation-lifetime/`. Affected validation passes
in 208.12 seconds with unchanged validated inputs and refreshed metadata.

These are proof-world primitives, not qualified allocator API calls. The existing
`proof_service_lifetime_effect_unsupported` admission blocker remains. Selected
API contracts, caller-owned initial ranges, complete allocator freshness,
logical/native generation correspondence and lifetime state across cutpoints
must still be connected to both exact and source call lowering. In particular,
Metapad's GlobalAlloc/GlobalFree path and caller lifetime remain unqualified.
No pilot rebuild or SDK repin is needed for this primitive batch.

External-service bindings now preserve their complete selected effect contract
through proof-call normalization and trusted adapter receipts. A synthetic
resolved-contract reproduction previously dropped a declared range release and
admitted it to the scalar-response oracle. It now fails before world construction
with `proof_service_lifetime_effect_unsupported`. Missing effect payloads,
allocation results and out-pointer ranges also fail closed; changing an effect
contract changes its proof-call identity. Python and jq both require
`declared_external_range_effects_checked`. A fresh normal-engine proof is accepted
by both readers; rehashed missing/false-policy mutations are rejected. The
typed-adapter reader also validates the retained effect payload, updated behavior
identity and renderer closure. Both readers reject rehashed missing/changed effect
contracts in that branch. The
69-test focused group passes in 7.35 seconds, and the retained normal-engine
proof takes 1.35 seconds. Final affected validation passes in 136.22 seconds
with unchanged validated inputs and refreshed metadata. The first affected run
passed but did not cover the old typed-receipt shape; the added direct reader
test and subsequent repair required this final validation. Evidence is under
`build/metapad-trial/proof-lifetime-effects/`. Historical receipts without this
policy are superseded for current authority. This closes metadata loss; the
allocation primitives described above are a subsequent step. Binding those
primitives to GlobalAlloc/GlobalFree contracts and checked caller ownership
remains integration work before Metapad can qualify. No pilot was rebuilt.

Native allocation rules now retain allocator-family and captured owner-word
metadata through checked profiles, site codecs and runtime lowering. The existing
CRT allocation/free rows use a separate family from HeapAlloc/HeapFree; the heap
pair checks argument zero before release reaches native callthrough. Wrong
owners, cross-family releases, interior/untracked owned pointers and unowned
overwrites fail closed. Failed releases preserve origins; address reuse under
a new owner cannot revive an old reference. Non-null zero-size owned allocations
remain releasable without granting byte access, while null results register
nothing. Eight new tests include five small CBMC queries and two removed-check
mutants. The 77-test focused group passes in 6.67 seconds; the complete generated
runtime with owner-tagged rules compiles for IA-32 without target execution or
link qualification. Affected validation passes in 279.98 seconds with unchanged
validated inputs and refreshed metadata. Evidence is under
`build/metapad-trial/range-ownership/`.
Metapad's installed source, intents and SDK pin remain unchanged. These are
native admission/lifetime checks, not a Portable-C allocation theorem: the
GlobalAlloc/GlobalFree contracts, proof-world allocation transitions and caller
lifetime composition remain required. No pilot was rebuilt for this batch.

Allocation authorities now bind repeated producer sites from one exact checked
contract instead of rejecting every multi-site allocator. The retained Metapad
transfer plan contains 23 resolved GlobalAlloc import call sites; its current callthrough row
still supplies no portable allocation theorem. Grouping checks contract identity,
profile binding and allocation shape, preserves each site's ABI frame and
physical provenance, and rejects conflicts or competing authorities. Runtime
origins remain per allocation instance; cross-site address reuse cannot revive
released references. Six new tests include three small CBMC queries, with a
regression mutant restoring the former one-site check. The 43-test focused group
passes in 4.00 seconds, and a complete generated runtime with grouped producers
compiles for IA-32 without execution or target-link qualification. Evidence is
under `build/metapad-trial/allocation-sites/`. Affected validation passes in
120.33 seconds with unchanged validated inputs and refreshed metadata. The
external trial source, intents and SDK pin are unchanged; no pilot was rebuilt.
The proof world still models invocation-scoped issued origins. Caller ownership,
allocation API contracts and general lifetime-bearing summaries remain required.

Native allocation-range registration now rejects generation exhaustion instead
of reusing a saturated generation. A retained production-body fixture reproduced
stale reference revival after release and address reuse at the counter limit;
the repair preserves live references, permits cleanup, and reports `0x2103`
before altering the range inventory. Five small CBMC queries pass, including an
arbitrary-counter case and a removed-guard mutant. The retained fixed case also
passes CBMC and compiles to an IA-32 PE32 fixture; that fixture was not executed
and is not a full runtime-link qualification. The 20-test focused group passes
in 1.31 seconds. Evidence is under `build/metapad-trial/allocation-lifetime/`.
The first affected run caught the runtime module's size limit; allocation
registration and generation helpers now live in `runtime_allocation_lifetime.py`,
with byte-identical emitted C verified after extraction. Final affected
validation passes in 122.72 seconds with unchanged validated inputs. This repairs one prerequisite for allocation
lifetime contracts without qualifying Metapad's heap or incoming views.
Release success is now explicit in checked machine-import and typed operation
contracts. Native range release checks the result and preserves live origins on
failure. Argument guards reject unsupported partial-release calls before native
callthrough; the existing VirtualFree row admits only size zero and exactly
MEM_RELEASE. The existing HeapFree, environment-string release and unmap rows
use nonzero success, while CRT free explicitly releases on normal return.
Missing conditions and profile disagreements fail closed. Eight new focused
tests include seven small CBMC queries and two removed-check mutants. A complete
generated runtime with release rules compiles for IA-32; it has not been linked
or executed as a qualified target. Evidence is under
`build/metapad-trial/release-contracts/`. The 74-test focused group passes in
3.36 seconds. Affected validation passes in 170.82 seconds with unchanged inputs
and current metadata, after declaring three profile resources omitted from the
initial filtered test source. No pilot was rebuilt; external trial files and
the SDK pin remain unchanged.
Portable-C allocator ownership, heap lifetime identity, partial-page transitions
and the connection to caller lifetimes remain unfinished. The selected
GlobalAlloc callthrough row still does not establish a portable allocation
theorem. See [the release contract](call-protocols.md#whole-range-release-conditions).

The public `expert semantic-diagnose --view callers` command now inventories
direct caller sites from retained machine IR, including captured inputs and
exact file-line/JSON-pointer locations. It binds input and site hashes, returns
explicit truncation, and reports unresolved indirect-call counts. It never
claims a closed caller set or proves origin, lifetime or reachability. Metapad's
three queried routines have five direct sites, including two omitted by the
earlier manual trace (`0xb131` and `0xb19c`). The normalizer at `0x56b5` still has
one recorded direct caller, `0x5c49`. The five sites match the independent
retained transfer-plan inventory. A warm public query takes 4.01 seconds;
independent retained GNU Hello input returns 40 sites in 5.02 seconds and
correctly reports truncation when limited to one. The 38-test focused group
passes in 2.76 seconds. Evidence is under `build/metapad-trial/caller-navigation/`;
affected validation passes in 126.22 seconds with unchanged validated inputs and
current generated metadata. No target was rebuilt or executed, and the
external trial's files and SDK pin remain unchanged. The caller-origin proof
