still needs to connect the existing runtime allocation/frame lifetime machinery
to the incoming views; the query itself does not discharge that obligation.

Typed proof service adapters now realize borrowed input references through the
same runtime hook as production before recording a call. This checks issued
origins, generations, permissions and reference bounds instead of accepting
arithmetic-valid metadata alone. Byte NUL inputs also require a readable zero at
the current visible view's final byte; a later repair cannot hide an invalid
call. This is a conservative termination witness, not first-NUL length semantics
or a caller-lifetime proof. Wider strings remain unsupported by this adapter.
Both receipt readers require `typed_service_borrowed_inputs_checked`; old
receipts remain superseded while pilot rebuilding is deferred. The 34-test
focused group passes in 6.33 seconds, including 18 small generated-thunk CBMC
queries capped at 30 seconds each and two removed-check mutants. Reference and
termination failures carry argument-specific operator guidance. Evidence is under
`build/metapad-trial/service-preconditions/`. Affected validation passes in
199.17 seconds; validated inputs stayed unchanged, metadata is refreshed, and
the installed trial files and SDK pin still match the preceding batch.
Metapad still needs caller-origin, target, output and callback contracts, and
continuation calls remain rejected. No pilot rebuild or SDK repin is needed for
this local prerequisite.
Static caller leads retained alongside the checks identify the count frame's
prologue at `0x56b5`, its direct caller at `0x5c49`, the incoming buffer slot at
caller EBP minus `0x14`, and the preceding internal routines at `0x5817` and
`0x55b7`. The first contains allocation, file-read and encoding-conversion calls.
These are investigation leads, not a checked caller-origin or alias relation.

Active native import conversion and Portable-C service admission now require
explicit `memory_effect` and `world_effect` categories. Physical ABI lowering
still accepts the four text-API frame declarations, but missing effect metadata
can no longer become the semantic claim `none`. The semantic frontend reports
`bound_external_call_effect_contract_missing`; overlay construction and the
active canonical native converter independently reject the same omission.
The earlier ABI-frame batch tested a legacy event converter, not this production
path. A retained-call intake probe confirmed that production had defaulted both
Metapad call contracts to no effects; the corrected probe rejects both in 1.68
seconds. It does not establish the indirect lstrlen target or qualify either API.
The 54-test focused group passes, as do three explicit-category/receipt tests.
Both proof readers require `machine_import_effect_categories_explicit`, so older
contextual receipts cannot satisfy the current policy. Evidence is under
`build/metapad-trial/call-contract-admission/`. String preconditions, output and
callback contracts and continuation-call support remain unfinished.
Final affected validation passes in 135.35 seconds. Its first run exposed older
service fixtures relying on absent effect categories; their intended read-only,
resource-output and callback-registration contracts are now explicit, with 11
additional fixture tests passing. Metadata is current. The external trial and
SDK pin are unchanged, and no full pilot was rebuilt.

Service transcripts now compare public memory at the call boundary using one
fresh symbolic byte observation per call. This avoids copying the world or
scanning buffers and prevents later stores from hiding a call-time difference.
Machine replay and typed source replay both check the recorded observation;
typed exact recording follows the same rule. Exposed caller-frame bytes are
included, while private-cell contracts remain separate. The comparison is
conservative over all public memory; narrowing it to checked callee footprints,
plus pointer lifetime/permissions, output effects and reentrancy, remains work.
Continuation calls are still rejected pending those contracts. Both receipt
readers require the new `call_public_memory_snapshots_checked` policy and typed
memory assertion coverage, superseding earlier qualification receipts.
The 47-test focused group passes in 2.99 seconds (11.23 seconds including Nix),
including eleven small CBMC runs capped at 30 seconds each. Positive replay,
hidden-difference, second-call, exposed-stack and removed-check cases exercise
the new boundary. Evidence is under `build/metapad-trial/call-memory/`.
Affected validation passes in 197.08 seconds, with generated metadata refreshed.
The external trial's recorded files and SDK pin remain unchanged; no full pilot
rebuild was run.

The kernel32/windowing profiles now contain ABI-only argument-frame declarations
for `lstrlenA/W` and `SetWindowTextA/W`. The pinned IA-32 MinGW headers and compiler
confirm one and two stdcall words respectively, including stack cleanup. Applying
the existing sparse-stack checker to Metapad's retained calls recovers offsets
`[0]` and `[0, 4]`; missing predecessor-staged words are already supported.
These declarations do not qualify memory, result or callback effects. The
original batch's rejection test used a legacy converter; the production-path
correction is recorded above. The indirect lstrlen target still
needs a checked binding. String-memory and window-message/reentrancy contracts,
then their continuation proof, remain required. Evidence is under
`build/metapad-trial/call-contracts/`; the external trial and SDK pin are unchanged.
The 12-test environment/header group and affected validation pass (83.38 seconds
for the latter). No full pilot/provider rebuild was run.

Continuation-dependent providers now declare a separate, unowned `exact_context`
semantic slice derived from their proof and linked transfer universe. Final
selection requires matching generated Behavioral-C definitions and their exact
dependency contracts. Candidate object admission and native receipt writing
recheck this requirement; dispatch input validation also compares it with the
proof's actual continuation inventory. Focused tests reject a second portable
replacement, missing/stale context, mismatching symbols and rehashed selections
with removed blockers. Strong dispatch/link receipts now record each context's
selected generated definitions, exact objects, unique strong symbols and linked
RVAs. Native realization retains the provider's context premise and checks that
the linked receipt discharges it. Conditional local proofs remain non-authorizing;
their checked provider facets require the explicit context, and only the final
selected link can authorize activation. A synthetic fixture passes the normal
proof engine and PE32 dispatch link with this boundary. Missing objects, weak or
duplicate symbols, changed selections and removed/rehashed context receipts fail
closed. Evidence is under `build/metapad-trial/conditional-link/`; this does not
qualify Metapad or establish a complete native application realization.
Final affected validation passes in 120.38 seconds, with metadata refreshed and
19 focused receipt/operator tests passing. The external trial is unchanged.
The 32-test focused group and affected validation pass (128.11 seconds for the
latter). Evidence is under `build/metapad-trial/exact-context-selection/`.
The external trial inputs and SDK pin are unchanged; no full pilot was rebuilt.

The toolkit now proves bounded shared continuation prefixes using the existing
`continuation_unit_ids` and unowned exact-C context. It executes the declared
exact units on both sides, checks effects and control, and requires complete
architectural state agreement at the resulting boundary, including exceptional
outcomes reached during the prefix. A register difference overwritten by the
prefix passes; exposing it through a store or failing to leave the prefix within
its bound is rejected. Context calls still require checked call contracts.
These local proofs remain non-authorizing until their exact continuation
selection dependency is discharged by the selected link. Both readers reject
missing context metadata/checks and forged local activation. Public draft review
preserves context separately from ownership.
The generic generated context code also passes Metapad's real first continuation
unit for differing arithmetic flags (2.23 seconds) and rejects an added ECX
difference (1.79 seconds). The external draft and its three blockers are unchanged;
the trial has not opted into a new context-dependent qualification. Evidence is
under `build/metapad-trial/common-continuation/`.
Affected validation passes in 210.52 seconds; the installed trial inputs and SDK
pin are unchanged. No full pilot/provider build was run for this batch.

Provider proof now checks architectural state at intrafunction continuation
exits, beyond the declared logical results. A tiny region that writes unbound
ECX previously passed with a matching EAX result; it now fails the required
`exit-continuation-state` assertion. Matching fallthrough and jump cases still
pass. Both Python and the strong jq reader require the new policy and assertion
inventory and reject the earlier unchecked receipt, even after self-digests are
rebuilt. This equality case does not discharge Metapad's call contracts or
provide a general contextual relation for dead state. Return and exceptional
boundary contracts remain separate. Historical direct/pilot receipts lack this
policy and cannot authorize the current checker. Evidence is under
`build/metapad-trial/continuation-state/`; no full pilot was rebuilt.
Affected validation passes in 184.99 seconds. The trial now pins repaired SDK
`/nix/store/mrgh4g1188scyhqzhz2cdn0bgm7vahii-source`; only the extractor lock input
changes. Public source checking passes in 7.10 seconds, and prerequisite checking
retains the three blockers in 0.98 seconds. Source and component intent are
preserved; there is no new provider or native activation receipt.

The continuation investigation reuses retained Metapad machine IR and exact-C
inputs without rebuilding the pilot. A generic entry-state query now uses the
existing dependency engine directly at a real unit, without synthetic transfers.
At RVA 0x5762 each of the five arithmetic flags closes within one unit. ECX
reaches two call-frame noninterference obligations across four units:
`kernel32.dll!lstrlenA` at 0x578b and `user32.dll!SetWindowTextA` at 0x580a.
A local paired exact-C check passes differing incoming flags while comparing
control, the full machine state and memory; changing ECX fails that comparison.
These are non-authorizing diagnostics. The call contracts and complete
continuation relation still need to enter normal provider qualification; the
three installed blockers remain. The entry query takes 4.74 seconds including
IR loading/adaptation; the positive and negative exact-C checks take 1.47 and
1.21 seconds. Evidence is under `build/metapad-trial/continuation/`.
The public `expert semantic-diagnose --view entry-dependencies` command now
performs the combined real-input query in 4.08 seconds without target evaluation;
public component checking displays the detailed three blockers in 0.99 seconds.
Affected validation for the query and public consumer passes in 162.56 seconds;
generated metadata is refreshed. Provider qualification remains open.

The preceding public Metapad integration installed the reviewed bisimulation intent,
source sync marker and three canonical write-fact references. One logical effect
can now retain multiple distinct machine facts; duplicate or unordered fact
references fail review. That checkpoint pinned SDK snapshot
`/nix/store/xwf023i0p5n1qvlf6j28vh81hdwfq1cv-source` and retains draft activation.
Public source checking passes (7.60 seconds initially, 1.58 seconds unchanged).
After adding blocker guidance to the binding alone, the source check returns the
same receipt in 2.36 seconds. Public qualification remains unavailable with three
explicit blockers: text-origin/lifetime, continuation-register relation, and the
caller-frame count object's ownership. Development status confirms those three
blockers but takes 80.19 seconds cold; that cost remains outside the interactive
budget. The historical direct proof is not an installed provider qualification.
Affected validation for this integration passes in 204.67 seconds, and the
separate repository/semantic-boundary group passes 37 tests.

The latest ASCII comparison rebuild takes 1,245.05 seconds and remains incomplete:
the scan shard passes, while the entry exit-value query times out at 300 seconds.
Its diagnostic receipt validated under its recorded v8 policy, but activation
remains false; it is not a current v9 receipt. Further real-pilot
runs are batched at integration checkpoints rather than after individual edits.

The earlier two-obligation Metapad success is superseded: the logical projection
merged its fixed count view with the text's NUL contract because both share a
physical pointer type. Value-specific logical types now preserve those contracts,
and both receipt readers reject the older policy. Entry passes again after also
fixing an aliased short NUL view that incorrectly shortened a fixed view. Entry
and scan now pass with checked view admission at the outgoing cut: non-nullness,
non-wrapping requested extent and, initially, exclusion of the private window
rebuilt from the captured outgoing ESP. Canonical shared-view admission now
allows declared views into that window while preserving fixed private objects
and checked image/stack separation. Resumption uses the same admission predicate. Both readers
accept the corrected direct evidence and reject a rehashed inventory with its
admission assertion removed.
They also reject the predecessor that checked the old window before ESP moved.
The normal cutpoint path now also checks the effective bytes of each captured
view over its full reference extent, including bytes beyond the visible prefix
and bytes currently classified as private. Both readers require those per-view assertions and reject the preceding
receipt without them. Captured access methods are now compared with a protected
entry-view snapshot at outgoing cuts; resumption assumes that same checked tuple.
The callback-clearing mutation is explicitly rejected at `scan:text`, with its
failing query taking 0.58 seconds instead of the earlier inconclusive memory-query
timeout. Both readers require the method assertions. That attempt checked
65 and 66 authored properties in 70 and 71 queries, but is now superseded by
required reference-metadata transport. Domain, generation, offset, permissions
and element width must reconstruct at each cut. A count-view permission mutation
that previously passed now fails explicitly at `scan:line_count`; both readers
reject inventories missing the metadata assertion. The unchanged draft's latest
10-second-budget checks pass entry but time out on scan memory equality; an
isolated replay succeeds in 12.17 seconds with a 30-second limit. Attempt fourteen
then satisfies both direct shards at that measured per-query budget: entry checks
67 authored properties in 72 queries, scan 68 in 73. That receipt is superseded
by the required callback-context relation: protected snapshots now check context
identity and contents, the complete runtime tuple, and full-width reference
addresses. A mutation of context permissions behind an unchanged pointer fails
at `scan:line_count`. The next canonical extent and caller-ownership relations
remain open. Final attempt sixteen satisfies entry with 69 authored properties
in 74 queries and scan with 70 in 75, retaining the measured 30-second per-query
budget. Both readers reject omitted context assertions and predecessor evidence.
Attempt sixteen is now superseded by required canonical extent transport and
full-reference memory checks. Final attempt eighteen satisfies entry with 71
authored properties in 76 queries and scan with 72 in 77. Requested, reference
and visible extents reconstruct under the current logical origin policy,
including NUL aliases, outgoing range admission and terminating bytes in both
worlds. Shrinking the count view and its callback context together is rejected;
bytes beyond the visible prefix also require equality. Both readers reject old
visible-only memory receipts and missing extent/reference-memory assertions.
This is direct diagnostic evidence, with production limits unchanged. It preserves
the existing modeled domain; it does not qualify the real caller's frame-relative
view or establish stack ownership.
A concrete canonical-transfer trace from RVA 0x56b5 reaches the selected entry
at 0x5731 with EBP−4 equal to ESP+8, inside the modeled private window. That real
entry path was excluded by the preceding view domain. Checked exposure of this
stack cell was therefore needed before caller qualification; its diagnostic
case and trace are retained under `build/metapad-trial/caller-frame/`.
The proof-world renderer now has an opt-in shared stack-span operation and an
explicit memory-range equality check. Solver regressions use the observed ESP+8
cell, preserve partial aliases and crossing writes, reject omitted shared writes
and source-frame clobbers, and compare prospective shared bytes while they are
still private. Spans must be registered before either side has effects; fixed
private objects cannot be exposed. The ordinary harness now derives spans from
canonical parameter projections and full reference extents, including NUL
aliases. Source-frame initialization must preserve borrowed bytes before the
resumed relation is assumed. Both readers require the per-function input-span
and frame-preservation checks; attempt eighteen is now stale.

The current direct Metapad check is attempt twenty: entry satisfies 78 authored
properties/83 queries, scan 79/84, with a longest query of 23.97 seconds at the
existing 30-second diagnostic limit. Distinct property/nonvacuity assertion names
resolve the duplicate-inventory failure recorded by attempt nineteen without
relaxing validation. A concrete scan replay with the count cell at ESP+8 passes;
omitting its source store retains a relation witness and fails exit public-memory
equality. Affected validation passes in 189.50 seconds and the additional codegen/
repository group passes 52 tests. Caller ownership and public qualification
remain open. Installed inputs and native overlay bytes are unchanged.
Inverse register-relative view-base restoration admits the count view at a cut.
The v9 safety query strategy selects static property IDs under consistent
instrumentation, preserving an independent unsliced unwind check. Focused
callback, invalid-pointer, bounds and unwind regressions pass, as do the strict
model/shard evidence reader and positive/negative Nix safety-selection checks.
There is no qualified provider world or object authority. The
installed binding's five blockers remain; caller/view-domain, continuation and
public qualification integration are still required. Evidence and scope are in
the [Metapad baseline](baselines/2026-09-05-interactive-lifting-baseline.md#metapad-direct-loop-proof).

Metapad now has a draft nine-unit, two-obligation loop proof plan with zero
enumerated paths. Scoped loading admits its compiled units while preserving the
unrelated global blocker and rejecting selected, overlapping or unscoped failures.
The installed target and its five blockers remain unchanged; the proposed loop
invariant is not yet proved.

The full contextual engine exposed a false acceptance of a declared register
result on fallthrough. Logical results are now compared on every normal region
exit, including continuations. Both receipt readers require the new result check
and the corrected partial-overlap cache policy. Historical pilot receipts must
be rebuilt; the focused normal-exit regression passes but does not discharge
the full current-tree validation or Metapad continuation-context obligations.

The actual Metapad region exposed a count/text alias mismatch in its entry-snapshot
state interface. The installed revision borrows the count word and reads it after
text normalization. Canonical configured-seed review and public source checking
pass, as does a bounded comparison of the generated region and revised source.
The unbounded regional proof and five explicit binding blockers remain open.
See the [alias-review evidence](baselines/2026-09-05-interactive-lifting-baseline.md#metapad-counttext-alias-review).

The latest unfamiliar-application follow-up resolves crossing private/public
writes in the sparse proof world and invalidates partially overwritten stack
cache entries. The full arbitrary-address frame-state fixture passes; a wrong
state increment rejects. This advances the Metapad blocker investigation but
does not qualify its full region or supersede the pilot requirements below.
See the [recorded checkpoint](baselines/2026-09-05-interactive-lifting-baseline.md#crossing-memory-stores-and-complete-frame-state-fixture).

The latest storage audit supersedes the earlier receipt checkpoints below.
A reassigned source context pointer was incorrectly reset when resuming a cut;
the resulting false acceptance is retained in
`build/cut-parameter-state/context-before.json`. The collector now includes
assigned or address-exposed automatic parameters. Current readers require
`source_cut_storage_overapproximated` and `source_cut_storage_inventory`, replacing
the local-only policy and input role. The storage-only scalar caller
qualified in 1,096.57 seconds under `build/cut-parameter-state/pilot/`, before
the argument-binding correction below.
The preceding remaining-pilot build finished in 864.45 seconds. Hello still times
out on exit value and `program_name`, jq on typed-call fields at position one,
and DX-Ball in all five nonempty safety partitions. Those receipts predate this
correction and can supply diagnostics only.

A subsequent parameter-binding audit also falsely passed when a constant local
was used as a logical parameter capture while the original C argument remained
live. Captures now bind to the compiler's actual argument identities and scopes;
renaming is supported, while substitution and shadowing reject. Each argument
requires an explicit relation. The new binding inventory and
`source_cut_parameter_bindings_checked` policy are mandatory. Evidence is under
`build/cut-parameter-binding/`. The argument-binding scalar build
qualified in 1,125.82 seconds under `build/cut-parameter-binding/pilot/`, before
the codec correction below.

The codec audit then found that a constant parameter encoding could still lose
the real argument value. Parameters now require the canonical reconstruction
encoding. Every source-state machine codec also checks its decoder round trip
on incoming transitions; both readers independently require those assertions.
A deliberately lossy codec falsely passes with the previous checks emulated,
and fails at the new round-trip assertion. Evidence is under
`build/cut-capture-codecs/`. Both readers reject the preceding full scalar receipt for its missing round-trip
checks. Affected validation passes in 177.00 seconds. The final current-policy
scalar rebuild finished in 1,084.57 seconds with complete qualification and both
regions satisfied. Both current receipt readers accept it; evidence is under
`build/cut-capture-codecs/pilot/`. This does not establish fresh native/link
activation or complete the other pilots.

Distinct per-cut local capture sets now pass a three-region solver fixture;
using a value captured only at an earlier cut or breaking the final capture
relation rejects. Interface parameters still require explicit reconstruction.
An unexpected-marker guard is shared between actual marker visits and a harmless
true harness call, preserving its assertion inventory when later resume skips
its lexical block. Actual visits retain the original alignment predicate.


2026-09-05 roadmap execution began with a frozen source baseline and a pinned
Metapad 3.6 unfamiliar-application trial. See
[`baselines/2026-09-05-interactive-lifting-baseline.md`](baselines/2026-09-05-interactive-lifting-baseline.md).
A new adversarial CBMC fixture reproduced connected replay accepting different
input bytes with equal event counts. The implementation now inventories an
independent input-memory equality goal using a pre-call arbitrary-byte snapshot,
including private storage exposed by logical references/views. This is a
transitional soundness repair; body-free summary composition, required pilot
proof discharge, and full roadmap completion remain outstanding.

The post-boundary-check pilot rebuild completed and its receipts were reopened:
jq remains qualified; Hello is violated at the connected input relation for
`memory-regions-equal`; DX-Ball still times out in all five nonempty language-safety
partitions. Replaying Hello's exact saved C inputs reproduces its counterexample.
The exact adapter constructs a fresh seven-byte span at address 4149283, while
the source supplies a subview of an origin at 2003201 with offset 2146082 and a
larger enclosing extent. Their effective address agrees; strict reference
metadata equality rejects the different representations. A checked view/origin
relation is required, retaining bounds, lifetime, aliasing, and memory checks.
This diagnosis does not authorize dropping metadata checks or activating Hello.

Connected fixed/value-bounded views now validate the requested range and realize
the incoming reference before resolving the callee's checked view. The adapter
rebuilds byte/span access contexts at that address and preserves strict summary
metadata equality. The proof world records issued reference metadata and rejects
forged bounds, missing permissions, foreign domains, stale generations, malformed
nulls, and invalid offsets; registry capacity is an asserted proof bound. Known
NUL-origin metadata is shared between the exact and source worlds. The historical
Hello input property passes a diagnostic replay in 86.57 seconds with these
changes. Focused world/region tests and both affected-validation passes succeed.
The fresh pre-policy-field provider build completed in 826.31 seconds: Hello's
connected input check passes, but its final `program_name` observable times out;
jq's typed call fields at position one also time out. Each is a 300-second limit,
not a counterexample, and both resulting qualifications are incomplete. Origin
records have subsequently been separated from the public-memory/event world
structure to avoid changing that structure's layout in unrelated proofs; 21
focused tests and affected validation pass after this adjustment. The final-policy
rebuild finished in 770.54 seconds: Hello still has two 300-second exit
value/observable timeouts, and jq still has a typed-call-fields timeout. Neither
qualification is complete. This remains the flat proof-world reference model;
heap/lifetime platform contracts remain unfinished.

A further adversarial solver fixture exposed an undeclared exact public write
being accepted at operation exit when declared results matched. Every exit now
requires effective public-memory equality, and the independently validated
required-assertion inventory and Python/Nix policy reject older receipts. The
fixture now rejects the write. All pilot receipts above predate this requirement
and cannot authorize the current policy.

The first body-free strategy handles stateless, service-free scalar operations.
Supplementary source certificates prove an empty write frame and equal results
for equal scalar inputs across independent contexts. Reachable undefined bodies,
loops, and recursive calls reject this initial acyclic strategy. The connected
provider path requires the qualified exact/source dependency and binds the
certificate to its actual source, interface, headers, symbols, and profile before
omitting the callee body. Paired wrappers retain input-memory and argument checks
and share an arbitrary result. The certificates are not independent authority.
Nine focused tests pass, including actual GOTO-body absence, mismatched-input
rejection, and stale-source/certificate mutations. The first real scalar child
certificate passed. The real connected-parent build finished in 256.01 seconds;
its qualification remains incomplete because both shards violate the scan
invariant. The trace gives zero input bytes nonzero abstract lower-case results
(128 in the entry shard, 161 in the sync shard), so the abstraction admits
continuing beyond a NUL terminator. The next summary increment needs a checked
postrelation; assuming zero preservation without proving it would be unsound.
Receipt-bound diagnostic GOTO reconstruction confirms both the exact and source
callee bodies are absent (7,283 entry instructions and 6,467 sync instructions).
Service-free adapters now supply the runtime context needed by paired wrappers.
Affected validation passes in 150.25 seconds. These results do not complete the
real-caller or callee-size scaling exits.
Its first build exposed a cost calculator that incorrectly counted backedges to
the starting cutpoint. Cost bounds now stop at the same barriers as exact slices;
an uncut cycle still rejects. General state, effect, lifetime, exceptional-outcome,
postrelation and scalability obligations remain open.

Scalar source certificates now carry optional proved predicates in the existing
Relation IR. Zero preservation is proposed generically for single-argument scalar
operations, then checked against the actual source; false candidates leave the
weaker summary intact. The reader checks the signature and reconstructs the
predicate harness, binding compilation inputs and checker options. Twelve focused
tests pass, including false-fact rejection, rehashed-predicate/command mutations,
and a size experiment: 256 additional source statements grow the checked callee
from 4 to 260 GOTO instructions while the paired parent remains at 1,158. This
experiment is non-authorizing and does not replace real-caller qualification.
The postcondition-enabled real caller build completed in 1,308.27 seconds, and
the hardened-policy rebuild completed in 1,415.89 seconds. Both entry and sync
shards are satisfied and `ascii-string-compare` qualification is complete. The
current readers accept the latter receipt and reject rehashed missing-signature,
missing-command, and unsatisfied-fact mutations. This resolves the recorded
zero-input loop-invariant failure for the scalar caller; it does not complete
general summary composition or establish interactive latency or native activation.
Evidence is under `build/scalar-summary-contracts/checked-postrelations/`.

The scalar strategy now omits replay branches for writes, calls, and atomics
that its checked empty frame and service-free interface prohibit. Argument,
input-memory, prefix, and call-pairing checks remain. The paired size fixture
shrinks from 1,158 to 1,122 instructions and retains the constant-size result
under callee growth. Successful proofs now retain their receipt-bound C inputs
as well as failures, enabling GOTO reconstruction; those files are proof-only,
non-authorizing artifacts. Focused checks pass, and affected validation including
this retention change passes in 135.94 seconds. Both real builds completed with
qualified callers, in 1,237.76 and 1,097.36 seconds respectively. The latest receipt
has 81/82 assertion properties and passes both readers and the negative mutations.
Reconstruction from its retained inputs yields 7,236/6,420 GOTO instructions and
confirms both callee bodies are absent. The corrected inspection preserves each
region's source/header directory; the earlier hash-only selection crossed region
boundaries and is superseded. Evidence is under `effect-free-replay/` and
`retained-models/corrected-goto-inventory/` in the same hierarchy. These scalar results meet the
corresponding caller/body-absence checks; general memory/state/effect summaries,
interactive latency, the remaining pilots, and linked/native activation remain
open.

Per-query timing records and path-qualified compile-input records now accompany
retained proof diagnostics. They preserve checker results and stay outside proof
authority. Corrected real-model replay and timing evidence are tracked under
`build/proof-query-timings/`. The corrected entry passes all 90 queries in
616.26 seconds, excluding compilation and nonvacuity; readiness, argument/memory
agreement, and exit value dominate the slowest queries. Affected validation
passes in 136.41 seconds. These measurements do not qualify a new native or
linked candidate or meet the interactive region budget. A joint-entry assertion
experiment times out at 300 seconds; the production query strategy is unchanged.

A subsequent cut-state audit found that an uncaptured local modified before a
cut could revert to its initializer in a resumed proof and falsely pass. The
checker now compiles a scope/write inventory, havocs visible mutable local
objects on resume, and restores the declared captures. Closed constants and
unchanged direct aliases of local objects may remain; other initializer values
are not assumed to survive. Ambiguous shadowed mutable storage fails closed.
Both readers require `source_cut_local_state_overapproximated: true`, so the
historical pilot receipts above are superseded for current authority. Fresh
qualification and validation of this change are tracked under
`build/cut-state-completeness/`. Affected validation passes in 142.21 seconds.
Both readers reject the historical receipt and a rehashed policy-only upgrade.
The fresh real-caller build under `pilot/` completed in 1,019.27 seconds with a
complete provider qualification and both regions satisfied. Its output was
`/nix/store/n9ap1giizdy70wbq62g6vf5aa3gjf82s-spaghetti-extractor-gnu-hello-ascii-string-compare-portable-c-work-package-provider-v2`.
It includes both the cut-local correction and distinct caller-context preservation.
At that checkpoint both readers accepted this receipt and rejected rehashed mutations
missing the policy or bound inventory (evidence under `build/cut-state-completeness/`).
The subsequent Hello, jq, and DX-Ball outcomes are recorded in the latest
checkpoint above and under `build/cut-state-completeness/remaining-pilots/`.
Downstream linked/native gates are still outstanding.

The scalar empty-frame wrapper also preserves each caller context instead of
copying the exact context into the source context. A solver regression reproduced
the old overwrite of a reserved field; the corrected wrapper retains protocol,
argument, memory and call-pairing checks. The context-only build completed in
1,044.15 seconds, but predates the cut-state policy and is not current authority.

Public `component check` now explains up to three failed obligations from a
sidecar bound to the selected qualification, with recorded source/proof locations
when available and relevant next steps. Missing, malformed or unrelated sidecars
never grant success. Thirty-nine CLI/operator tests pass; affected validation
passes in 101.25 seconds (`build/operator-proof-diagnostics/`). Historical Hello
and jq timeout displays were also checked without treating those proofs as
current authority. Workspace source mapping and automatic proposals remain open.

Authored acyclic cuts now use the generated, mapped exact-C region path and the
existing checked source-capture mechanism. A second adversarial fixture reproduced
missing public-memory agreement at sync termination. Syncs now require effective
public-memory equality and connected-call pairing before terminating a shard;
aggregate evidence rejects omission of these required checks. Generated two-region
solver fixtures reject incorrect live values, incorrect suffixes, and omitted
branch alternatives. Automatic proposals, measured region budgets, full pilot
migration, and broader stage-2 exits are not yet complete.

Sync intents can also bind captures to explicit source member paths such as
`context->state.cursor`. The bindings are content-bound, permit only named
objects and direct/pointer members, and retain the normal pointer-validity and
resumption proofs. Generated-C fixtures pass for struct and pointer members and
reject a null base. This removes a bare-variable-only authoring restriction.
The DX-Ball migration still needs a checked relation for state represented in
`context->state` and exported by the production overlay after the source call;
ordinary physical-memory equality alone does not model that representation.

The proof theorem and authority boundary remain viable, but the real pilots
show that the current monolithic obligation strategy is not yet the scalable
endpoint. Width-aware write events, longest-acyclic-path capacity bounds, the
repaired affine exact-stack cache, stable closure-wide service identities,
runtime-side-aware typed record/replay, and locally asserted model validity are
now generic. The global `overflow == 0` goal has been removed: capacity,
transcript shape, service context/spec/index, memory readability/writability,
and atomic bounds are independently inventoried assert-then-assume proof goals.
No timeout or incomplete receipt grants activation authority.

The last pre-v8 GNU Hello `program-name-selection` receipt proves nonvacuity, all
five nonempty paired language-safety partitions, continuous exact transfer,
and exit control, target, and value. Its final `program_name` state equality is
the sole 300-second timeout. This is no longer the earlier typed-argument
mismatch; the parent formula is re-executing the already-proved
`memory-regions-equal` dependency and its nested service transcript.

The jq `output-value-pipeline` pilot is now satisfied and activation-authorized.
Its authoritative provider is
`/nix/store/k58bmgs1kdlqiabsyrgvh962a3fmjs67-spaghetti-extractor-jq-jq-output-value-pipeline-portable-c-work-package-provider-v2`;
the target contextual gate is
`/nix/store/j75js4niqra4fh7n4msasiwi0xzj0aim-jq-output-value-pipeline-contextual-bisimulation-v6`.
All qualification facets are checked and the strong proof has no blockers.
The v8 proof replaces per-field typed-service solver runs with one required
`typed-call-fields` assertion at each complete call boundary. Both call
boundaries pass, along with all language-safety, exit, capacity, call replay,
and exact-stack compatibility goals. The model retains two calls, one declared
public write slot, a 17-write private bound, and 14 affine stack accesses.

The last pre-v8 DX-Ball `directdraw-init` receipt remains fail-closed before its
semantic assertions: all five nonempty paired language-safety partitions hit
the 300-second limit. The single acyclic entry shard contains 65 exact units,
11 possible calls, 16 public write slots, a 60-write private bound, and no
affine stack cache. Replacing 28 path-shaped proofs with one monolith removed
path enumeration but overcorrected; large acyclic regions need principled
barriers too, not only cyclic SCCs.

The next simplification boundary is therefore explicit: consume an already
qualified connected definition through a proof-carrying relational summary
rather than inlining its body; generate exact-write-specific stack-cache
handlers so known affine private writes do not traverse generic history; and
insert or require cutpoints for large acyclic regions based on service/effect
boundaries and a checked size budget. These reuse the existing typed event,
cutpoint, and receipt formats. They do not require a second semantic IR, path
enumeration, another solver, or a source `init`/`step`/`finish` API.

Completion requires satisfied strong contextual receipts for every activated
pilot, exact linked-object/dispatch evidence in a real Portable-C candidate,
and all applicable gates. A realized derivation, passing runtime test, source
receipt, or partially satisfied shard is not completion.

# Previous Completed Goal: Faithful Semantic-Module Deployment and Incremental Idiomatic Lifting

## Historical authority note

Everything below this heading records the previous completed goal and its
2026-09-02 checkpoints; it is not the current execution contract or current
target status. That completed goal did not replace or narrow
[`unified-behavioral-c-lifting-native-ingress-deployment-plan.md`](unified-behavioral-c-lifting-native-ingress-deployment-plan.md).
Its detailed plan remains the historical specification and evidence journal.

## Objective

Implement and verify every requirement in the unified Behavioral-C lifting,
native-ingress, and deployment plan through all staged clean cuts. Deliver a
fast automatic faithful-C path for the whole conservative semantic universe
and a practical operator workflow that replaces small and medium semantic
slices with independently qualified idiomatic C. Preserve honest blockers for
jq and DX-Ball rather than manufacturing authority.

The goal is incomplete until the exact deployed candidates and observed
project distributions satisfy every required semantic, provider, runtime,
ingress, TLS, SEH, composition, deployment, observation, performance,
metadata, architecture, absence, Nix, and applicable Wine gate.

## Keystone and canonical semantic boundary

The keystone is `linked-semantic-module-v2` over
`executable-transfer-plan-v2`, with the following exact interpretation:

1. `executable-transfer-plan-v2` is the only executable semantic language.
2. `semantic-object-v1` is a content-addressed relocatable package over that
   language. It may package or reference transfer bodies, symbols, roots,
   relocations, objects, environment contracts, and provider requirements; it
   must not translate function behavior into a second semantic IR.
3. A packaged `semantic-object-v1` is the sole production input to the direct
   conservative semantic linker.
4. `linked-semantic-module-v2` records the deterministic may-realization
   universe, stable definitions, typed relocations, genuine semantic holes,
   typed residual runtime obligations, and only canonical site-local
   frontiers. It does not embed a second copy of executable semantics.
5. Whole-program must-provenance, solvers, emulators, fuzzers, CBMC examples,
   differential execution, and tests remain analysis or veto mechanisms.
   Their failure cannot remove may-reachable behavior, grant authority, or
   change production module identity.
6. Generated Behavioral C explicitly owns every original definition in the
   faithful configuration. Qualified portable providers may replace exact
   content-addressed slices in hybrid configurations. No selection may fall
   back silently.

This is one semantic language, one relocatable packaging boundary, one
production link algorithm, one total provider selection, and one native
realization/deployment chain. It is not permission to create a universal
artifact containing analysis, source presentation, runtime state, and PE
layout.

## Non-negotiable invariants

- Preserve fail-closed authority and veto-only diagnostics.
- Preserve the complete root, direct-edge, exception, outcome, relocation,
  object, and provider-requirement universes under migration and broadening.
- Distinguish semantic closure, executable qualification, and deployed
  completion in every status surface.
- Bind every selected source and object to the pinned compiler, checker,
  linker, realization, deployment, and candidate hashes.
- Keep PE layout, ingress bridges, TLS, SEH, loader surfaces, and candidate
  observation downstream of semantic linking.
- Keep generated faithful C immutable. Operator-authored C lives only in a
  separately hashed source package and qualifies only its selected slice.
- Migrate every in-tree consumer before retiring a production format, reader,
  constructor, input, command, or compatibility path.
- Prefer the existing Python implementation, Nix content-addressed phases,
  and measured algorithmic improvements. Add a dependency, cache, daemon,
  proof format, or Rust kernel only after a real vertical demonstrates that it
  is necessary.
- Tests and evaluator comparisons can veto a claim but cannot authorize one.

## Current checkpoint

The semantic-object, direct conservative V2 linker, total V2 provider
selection, native realization, generic ingress/runtime, real GNU Hello
faithful deployment, Hello-derived callback registration, and comprehensive
synthetic PE32 DLL/Wine verticals exist. Production semantic linking no longer
runs the optional must fixed point. The exact libjq vertical demonstrated that
the simplified linker and machine-IR exporter scale without another cache or
artifact system.

The architecture and target migrations are now closed through work item D.
The active work is repository closure: refresh the evidence surfaces, prove
that the remaining retired paths are absent, and pass the exact root and target
flake checks without weakening any honest blocker.

2026-09-01 canonical-spine checkpoint: work item A is complete. The production
Nix linker constructor now accepts only `semanticObject` and `namePrefix`; the
original PE, behavioral roots, must-worklist limit, CPU budget, and prepared
link budget exist only on the separately scheduled developer performance
derivation. Architecture tests enforce the one-path Python and Nix APIs,
forbid decoder/transfer/PE/solver dependencies in the direct linker, and keep
linked definitions as hashes and dependency contracts rather than copied
transfer bodies. Focused semantic-object, linked-module, Behavioral-C
differential, SDK, metadata, and retired-architecture gates pass.

The exact real-scale outputs retain their canonical meaning: GNU Hello is
complete with 8,019 definitions, 12,249 active relocations, 109 residual
obligations, and zero holes or frontiers. After the jq capability migration,
libjq has 36,304 active symbols, 51,809 relocations, 412 obligations, 607
honest holes, and zero frontiers. The four exact x87 forms subsequently
selected for native exact replay reduce that libjq inventory to 603 holes
without changing its roots, edges, relocations, or obligations. The jq root
has 14 roots, 5,709 direct edges,
7,026 relocations, 89 obligations, and 21 honest holes.

2026-09-02 repository-closure checkpoint: GNU Hello's faithful deployment and
all three qualified replacements pass, jq's two-module migration passes with
its target-owned blockers unchanged, and DX-Ball passes through the common
semantic-module, service-transducer, runtime, native-module, deployment, and
observation path. DX-Ball has all eleven DirectDraw-tail services bound and
its contextual checker reaches both target-owned success-tail calls. The

The Hello semantic-link performance gate lives in `targets/flake.nix`, where

canonicalization, component services/refinement/contracts, and environment

repository ceilings. Production lint, Python closure, repository-boundary,
