A reversible comment-only probe in `semantic_path_operations.py` tested the
actual reuse boundary after warming the jq aggregate. Nix advertised 178
floating content-addressed derivations, but the real build completed in 0.83
seconds and executed exactly one cheap checked-boundary builder. No original
extraction, ISA qualification, machine IR, transfer plan, semantic object,
linked module, Behavioral-C, or runtime builder ran. Reverting the comment and
refreshing metadata restored the canonical jq output. This closes the measured
invalidation requirement: the remaining source-closure over-invalidation is
small and does not justify a cache, daemon, alternate IR, or new artifact.

## Ordered active work

### A. Freeze the canonical spine

- Prove with architecture tests that the semantic object does not introduce a
  second operation language and that the linked module does not duplicate
  transfer bodies.
- Require the production linker to consume only the semantic object and to
  import no decoder, solver, PE parser, evaluator, or must-worklist engine.
- Require every linked definition to have deterministic root, relocation, or
  runtime-dependency inclusion provenance.
- Require diagnostic inputs and budgets to be identity-neutral for the linked
  module.
- Require production Behavioral-C, provider, realization, and deployment
  consumers to enter through the semantic object or linked module rather than
  independently adapting machine IR or transfer semantics.

Exit: the focused semantic-object, linked-module, Behavioral-C differential,
metadata, and retired-architecture checks pass, and the real Hello and libjq
module outputs retain their expected identities.

Status: complete on 2026-09-01. Work item B is active.

### B. Close the operator-first GNU Hello vertical

- Rebuild and observe the faithful generated-C candidate.
- Rebuild, qualify, realize, deploy, and observe the independent
  `ascii-to-lower`, `program-name-selection`, and
  `startup-callback-registration` portable replacements.
- Preserve the Hello-derived callback-registration and synthetic
  `encapsulated_owned`/PE32 DLL coverage.
- Verify exact slice ownership, total provider selection, pinned tool and
  object binding, deployed candidate hashes, and observation results.
- Verify that a failed portable provider leaves the explicit faithful
  configuration buildable and that an unrelated change reuses a
  byte-identical qualification.
- Enforce the existing wall-time budgets; treat RSS as generous telemetry.

Exit: the public workflow demonstrates faithful deployment followed by three
independently proved and deployed idiomatic replacements without whole-program
must-provenance closure.

Status: complete on 2026-09-01. The complete `target-gnu-hello` gate passes.
It builds and observes the faithful generated-C candidate, the independently
qualified `ascii-to-lower`, `program-name-selection`, and
`startup-callback-registration` portable replacements, and the derived
registration candidate under Wine. Exact provider selection and native
realization checks pass for every configuration. The isolated real-scale
semantic-object replay remains within its eight-second wall-time budget; a
concurrent full-target run exceeded that budget only under unrelated build
contention and the isolated gate was therefore retained unchanged. Work item C
is active.

### C. Migrate jq as a target-owned multi-module project

- Model `jq.exe` and `libjq-1.dll` as two target-owned semantic modules and
  bind their code, data, forwarder, delay-import, loader-service, deployment,
  and observed-hash edges.
- Resolve reusable CRT/Win32 import contracts, physical call protocols,
  environment facts, and provider identities outside target-local code.
- Rebuild after each capability and prove that only the intended stable hole
  identities disappear while the conservative semantic universe is
  preserved.
- Qualify useful jq slices when their local boundaries close; do not require
  whole-program jq completion to support operator-authored idiomatic C.
- Preserve honest `jv`, refcount, loop, callback, ownership, and external
  environment blockers.

Exit: jq uses the same V2 semantic-module/provider/realization/project path as
Hello, with exact candidate-observed multi-image bindings or a stable honest
blocker for every incomplete requirement.

Status: complete on 2026-09-01. One reusable reviewed profile extension now
covers the previously missing public Win32/MSVCRT calls, and a separate
Oniguruma profile covers its pinned public depth-limit API. The root jq module
retained all roots, direct edges, relocations, and residual obligations while
its holes fell from 105 to 21; libjq retained the same conservative universe
and obligations while its holes fell from 659 to 607, then to 603 when the
same four independently qualified x87 forms used by DX-Ball became exact
native-replay obligations. The three service frames
for `jv_copy`, `jv_free`, and `jv_dumpf` now bind to their existing generated
public-header ABIs as imports rather than being mislabeled as direct main-image
functions. The public project surface exports linked-module packages instead
of invalid content-addressed interior paths. A target-owned migration gate
pins the remaining three private libjq ABI blockers, both incomplete target
environments, all three corresponding cross-image edge blockers, and the exact
conservative counts. The complete jq target gate passes; authored jq components
remain honestly blocked on semantic and service/callback/outcome review. Work
item D is active.

### D. Migrate DX-Ball through reusable loader and object capabilities

- Resolve executable jump-table and relocation recovery without pruning the
  exact universe.
- Complete checked COM/vtable services, resources, code/data imports,
  forwarders, object selectors, callbacks, shared data, TLS/DLL lifecycle,
  SEH/nonlocal outcomes, and multi-image relationships through generic
  contracts and fixtures.
- Qualify `directdraw-init` only after its complete local service and ownership
  boundary is checked.
- Exercise each new capability in the synthetic PE32 project before relying on
  it for DX-Ball.

Checkpoint (2026-09-01): the environment resolver now accepts exactly the
canonical empty EAT emitted for an executable with no export directory; all
contradictory partial EATs still fail closed. More importantly, target
cutpoint materialization and executable-data/overlapping-decode
classification now form one conservative fixed point. The original cutpoint
pass had frozen finite-control recovery before classification exposed the
complete selector domains of DX-Ball's optimized CRT `memcpy`/`memmove`
tables. The coupled pass materializes 21 additional qualified units at 12
exact targets, reaches a stable classification with zero conflicts, and
leaves no recovered jump table with an incomplete unit binding. Roots remain
14 while direct edges grow from 11,577 to 11,591 and active symbols from 9,255
to 9,269; all 38 derivative relocation holes disappear, reducing semantic
holes from 53 to 15 without changing the 259 residual runtime obligations.
The complete DX-Ball target gate passes and a target-owned migration
checkpoint pins this conservative universe. The remaining environment
blockers are exactly `UnhandledExceptionFilter` and `RtlUnwind`; the latter
must become a checked SEH/nonlocal runtime obligation rather than an ordinary
returning import.

Checkpoint (2026-09-01): those two imports now pass through one closed checked
external-service seam in the existing machine profile, resolved environment,
linked semantic module, and runtime plan. `RtlUnwind` has an exact four-word
stdcall frame with a declared `nonlocal` outcome, target-frame and checked
code-capability operands, x86 SEH unwind behavior, and abandoned-lifetime
cleanup. It is never represented as an ordinary host call from the private
runtime stack. `UnhandledExceptionFilter` has the exact pointer-to-
`EXCEPTION_POINTERS` view, nested exception-record and x86 `CONTEXT` views,
same-thread registered-filter relationship, and loader-owned fallback. The
resolved environment is consequently complete, and the linked module retains
the same 14 roots, 11,591 direct edges, 9,269 active symbols, and 12,287 active
relocations. The former service holes become two typed residual runtime
obligations, increasing that inventory from 259 to 261, while holes fall from
15 to four. All four remaining holes are exact qualified-platform gaps:
`fcos` at RVA `0xd6cc`, `fsin` at `0xd6d9`, `fistp qword` at `0xe569`, and
`fdiv qword` at `0x10145`.

This checkpoint does not claim native service realization. The runtime plan
emits one exact `external_service_runtime_unsupported` blocker for each checked
service until the generic SEH/nonlocal and exception-object implementations
exist. Malformed obligations fail closed. The complete DX-Ball target gate and
focused external, semantic-link, and canonical-runtime suites pass with these
honest blockers.

Checkpoint (2026-09-01): all four remaining x87 holes now use the existing
qualified-platform exact native-replay path. The selected target occurrence
set contains exactly four native replays; the wider platform selection retains
27 non-active Lean-form issues as honest diagnostics rather than letting them
block these exact occurrences. DX-Ball's linked semantic module is therefore
complete with 14 roots, 11,591 direct edges, 9,269 active symbols, 12,287
relocations, 261 typed obligations, and zero holes. This is semantic
completion, not native completion.

The callback-capability projection now preserves a resource lifetime's exact
`end_event` in the content-bound obligation and rejects a missing event. The
runtime planner consequently reaches the two checked external services rather
than failing on a malformed callback lifetime. `ExitProcess` is bound to the
original loader-written Kernel32 IAT slot through the target's unified support
policy. An incomplete runtime now emits only checked diagnostic plans and an
incomplete non-authorizing package; it generates no C or assembly and cannot
enter a link receipt. The target gate pins that behavior and the exact two
remaining blockers: `win32-rtl-unwind-v1` and
`win32-unhandled-exception-filter-v1`.

The runtime plan now projects those services directly from the linked
semantic module into one compact content-bound `external_service_routes`
inventory. It contains two protocol routes and only the three real DX sites:
two for `RtlUnwind` and one for `UnhandledExceptionFilter`. Blockers derive
from that same inventory, and a ready runtime rejects any route until its
generic implementation is linked. This establishes the dispatch seam without
duplicating service semantics or treating the expanded indirect-call table as
authority.

Because that inventory is a required contract field, the runtime plan made a
clean V5-to-V6 cut rather than mutating V5 in place. Every in-tree producer,
validator, provider, realization, target, and synthetic/Hello consumer now
uses V6, and V5 is a retired registry tombstone. The complete Hello/Wine, jq,
and DX-Ball target gates pass after the cut.

The current runtime plan also exposes a speed defect, not a semantic defect:
each indirect external site materializes the Cartesian product of the site and
every admitted loader target, producing roughly 14,000 repeated rows for this
module. A small environment change consequently reevaluated a wide 123-phase
closure. The V6 format-only clean cut also scheduled 236 Hello and 228 combined
jq/DX derivations and unnecessarily rebuilt the qualified-platform release and
Behavioral-C providers. The clean optimization is to retain one global
content-addressed
external target/contract catalog and let each site name a compact checked
domain reference. It must preserve the same may-domain and cannot introduce a
cache, alternate IR, or target-local pruning.

Checkpoint (2026-09-01): the generic service realization is now complete for
this migration seam. The synthetic PE32 DLL/Wine vertical installs guest SEH
records on the captured invocation stack, virtualizes the guest TEB exception
head separately from the native gateway chain, invokes younger unwind handlers
with checked exception/context objects, applies abandoned-frame cleanup, and
resumes only the exact authorized continuation. DX-Ball's two `RtlUnwind`
sites and one `UnhandledExceptionFilter` site consequently select checked
runtime implementations; its runtime plan is ready with zero blockers. Exact
overlapping transfer entry slices at one callback instruction are coalesced
only when their contract, lifetime, invocation, argument, and target authority
are byte-identical; disagreement still fails closed.

The external-callthrough normalization is also complete as a clean V6-to-V7
cut. V7 stores one content-addressed target-contract catalog, one deduplicated
checked-domain table, and one physical site row per occurrence. Validation
losslessly reconstructs and checks the former site-by-target relation before
runtime lowering, so no target was pruned and no second authority was added.
On DX-Ball the plan fell from 115,277,024 bytes to 10,851,144 bytes: 426
physical sites, 286 target contracts, and 92 domains preserve all 25,064
site-target pairs. The exact format, counts, and a 16-MiB ceiling are target
gates. The native PE32/Wine fixture and complete DX-Ball gate pass.

A controlled target-presentation probe changed only `targets/dxball/default.nix`.
The transfer plan, semantic object, linked module, qualified platform,
qualified runtime provider, and every shared ISA derivation retained the same
derivation identities; only target lint/regression leaves changed. Nix's
floating content-addressed dry-run still advertises the full 123-node graph,
but an unchanged build completed in 0.09 seconds without executing a builder.
That display is not an invalidation count and does not justify another cache or
source-closure system. The existing role-checked Python module closures and
Nix content addressing are the correct reuse mechanism.

Checkpoint (2026-09-01): the V6 component work package now turns the formerly
opaque machine-projection blocker into a content-bound, non-authorizing review
frontier without adding a format, semantic language, or provider path. The
frontier is projected solely from `executable-transfer-plan-v2`, is embedded
inside the existing blocker, and inventories exact call events with their
dependency-closed expression nodes, memory-write effects, and control
outcomes. Its parser checks the nested hashes, coverage, ordering, counts, and
explicit policy. Exact normalized import-name matches are presentation-only;
they neither populate `service_bindings` nor reduce the blocker.

The real `directdraw-init` frontier exposes a boundary mismatch that must be
resolved before qualification. The selected operation is 86 transfers, not a
narrow DirectDraw initializer: it contains 38 calls (18 named external, 17
indirect, and three internal), 123 writes, and the preceding Win32 window
construction imports `LoadIconA`, `LoadCursorA`, `GetStockObject`,
`RegisterClassA`, `CreateWindowExA`, `UpdateWindow`, and `SetFocus`, in addition
to `DirectDrawCreate` and the error paths. The authored C and interface model
only the DirectDraw tail and accept a window as an input, while the selected
machine region creates that window itself. The target gate pins these facts
and all six declared services remain unmapped. This is an honest component
boundary/design blocker, not a missing runtime primitive, and the complete
DX-Ball gate still passes.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): the operator boundary decision is now explicit. The
authored DirectDraw-only component retains its narrow interface and source;
its machine binding is reselected from the full 86-transfer window-creation
procedure to the unique 65-transfer suffix beginning at RVA `0xcd5c`, whose
first event is `DirectDrawCreate`. The only incoming direct edge is the
excluded prefix edge from `0xcd5a`. The suffix has 27 calls, 83 writes, six
return exits, and two internal calls. A separate exact blocker inventories the
captured-frame/register live-ins, global window/interface object selectors,
return projections, internal effects, and complete interface-service bindings
still needing review. The original machine-effect blocker and all six unmapped
authored services remain; no portable authority was inferred. The complete
DX-Ball target gate passes with these facts pinned.

The common runtime now obtains checked interface-method targets only from the
content-bound callable domain. Factory and method out-interface relations
register exact live object/vtable/class instances; indirect callthrough checks
the live receiver, vtable slot, class, generation, and release lifecycle. A
physical indirect site is correctly modeled independently of whether a domain
member is a loader import or interface method. Serialized target evidence
references the canonical method digest instead of embedding another copy,
reducing the real DX runtime plan from 18,203,481 to 15,116,243 bytes and
restoring the 16-MiB gate.

Callback-bearing interface methods are deliberately not claimed yet. They
remain in the canonical callable domain and produce 20 exact
`interface_callback_callthrough_runtime_unsupported` blockers for the four
DirectDraw enumeration operations across five interface generations. The
incomplete runtime package emits only checked plans and no C or assembly; the
qualified provider preserves those diagnostics instead of crashing or
pruning methods. The standalone PE32 ingress/Wine suite and focused canonical
runtime suites pass after making their isolated shared-runtime seam explicit.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): the non-callback interface seam now closes through a
real synthetic PE32 provider DLL and the deployed candidate. The original and
candidate `fixture.dll` both import `FixtureCreate` through the loader-written
IAT. The checked factory relation registers a live `IFixture`; generated C then
dispatches `Clone`, `SetValue`, `GetValue`, and `Release` through the exact
vtable/class/generation catalog, registers the nested clone, and rejects a
cached `GetValue` capability after release before the provider is invoked.
The Wine host pins the complete provider trace for both the successful and
stale paths, not merely their return values.

The same vertical found and fixed three generic runtime defects rather than
adding a fixture-specific bridge: interface method identities were being
dropped from the outgoing bridge table, wildcard physical-frame rows competed
with exact callable targets, and saved caller state was addressed relative to
each transfer's changing input stack rather than its stable captured base.
The final gates bind one interface catalog with four methods, seven typed
indirect-callthrough obligations, all 76 site-target pairs, 71 selected
definitions, 11 runtime obligations, the preserved factory import, 17 native
bridges, the composed PE, and the observed deployment hash. The focused
runtime tests and complete native-module Wine/deployment/observation gate pass.

The binary-producing fixture remains deliberately input-addressed with fixups
disabled. Floating content-addressed finalization can rewrite an embedded
output path in PE/COFF auxiliary bytes after the fixture has hashed the exact
module, invalidating its own receipt. All semantic phases above those exact
binary bytes remain content-addressed. This is an integrity boundary, not a
second cache or semantic path.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): callback-bearing interface callthrough is now closed
through that same generic fixture and runtime. Header-derived callback
signatures lower to checked PE32 physical frames; unsupported aggregate,
floating, wide, narrow, or unknown callback results remain fail-closed rather
than being guessed as EAX. The provider publishes an interface callback,
re-enters the candidate on the same host thread, receives the checked result,
and an invocation-lifetime callback retained past the call is rejected. Exact
object/vtable ranges created for the temporary interface instance are released
with the same generation; pre-existing ranges are reused without being
silently overwritten or released.

`module-runtime-plan-v8` represents interface publication authority once per
canonical method contract instead of once per site/member pair. Ordinary
callback-registration authority remains site scoped. DX-Ball consequently has
764 checked-site publications and 20 interface-method publications rather than
5,824 expanded rows. A second lossless normalization factors the identical
9,022-RVA callback target set out of 49 checked contracts into one
content-addressed callback-target domain. Validation reconstructs each
original contract and verifies its original digest before lowering. No target
or site-target pair is removed.

The exact DX-Ball runtime plan is ready with zero blockers, 426 physical
sites, 1,408 target contracts, 92 checked domains, all 167,558 site-target
pairs, 13 physical callback domains, 117,286 domain-target memberships, 784
publications, and one callback-target domain. Its serialized size falls from
20,259,876 bytes before publication normalization to 15,524,398 bytes, below
the existing 16-MiB veto. The runtime package emits its complete source set.
The native PE32 DLL/Wine deployment and observation fixture, focused runtime
and environment suites, DX-Ball target gate, format registry, metadata,
import-smoke, and retired-architecture gates pass. V7 is retired after the
same-cut consumer migration.

This also makes the next performance boundary explicit. DX-Ball runtime
qualification remains a serial full-CPU phase taking roughly two to three
minutes with about 4.2 GiB RSS. Memory is acceptable; the dominant work is the
117,286 native domain-target trampolines and their generated artifacts,
including a roughly 56-MiB ingress source and 170-MiB runtime object. Any next
optimization must preserve the exact may-domain and use the existing plan,
Nix content addressing, and generic runtime rather than adding a cache or a
parallel semantic system.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): the existing V6 work package now makes the next
`directdraw-init` review mechanical without granting authority. It derives
presentation-only interface-method candidates from the linked module's
already admitted callable domains only when an indirect target is exactly the
syntactic vtable form `load(add32(receiver, constant-slot-offset))`. It claims
no receiver provenance and labels its confidence
`exact_syntactic_vtable_slot_offset_only`. The 65-transfer suffix has fourteen
indirect calls; seven have this form and produce 130 candidate method rows over
slots 16, 24, 32, 44, 80, and 112. The other seven read `ESI` directly and are
the captured `ShowWindow` live-in, confirming that the suffix boundary still
needs an explicit input contract. The authored component also omits checked
`GetCaps`, `CreateSurface`, `CreateClipper`, `SetHWnd`, `SetClipper`, and two
internal effects, and its HRESULT-shaped returns disagree with the machine
procedure's zero/failure and one/success outcomes. Qualification therefore
remains correctly blocked.

The runtime code generator now factors repeated realization storage without
changing the semantic plan or virtual bridge identities. Per-target RVAs and
capability generations remain dense; full ingress descriptors are stored once
per distinct frame/lifecycle/outcome class, their 117,286-target mapping is 39
ranges, interface callback arguments are nine domain ranges, and mutable
capability state is zero-initialized BSS with immutable lifetime ranges. On the
complete DX-Ball gate this reduces `native-ingress-runtime.c` from 30,860,315
bytes and 679,342 lines after the first descriptor-only factoring to 5,836,877
bytes and 409,653 lines, and its MinGW object from 156,594,016 to 2,391,618
bytes. Descriptor storage is 27 while the exact virtual target and capability
count remains 117,286. The full target still took 181 seconds versus the prior
174-second sample, so compiler/object bloat is closed but end-to-end latency
has not materially improved; the next performance work must profile the
remaining derivation rather than infer success from artifact size.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): phase profiling then identified the architectural
latency defect. `module-runtime-plan-v8` was compact on disk, but runtime
validation and range planning each expanded its 426 sites and 1,408 target
contracts back into 167,558 rows and reparsed the same checked contracts. The
runtime now retains one validated in-memory catalog/domain view and passes it
directly between those consumers. Target contracts are parsed once per site
kind; the compatibility expansion remains only for tests and developer
inspection. This introduces no new format, cache, or authority and preserves
the public plan bytes exactly.

On the exact DX-Ball linked module, standalone runtime rendering fell from
142.399 seconds to 111.515 seconds (21.7%) while retaining the same ready
status and roughly 5.5 GiB working set. The rebuilt qualified-runtime provider
resolved to the exact pre-change content-addressed output
`/nix/store/za45cya0h4139yyjs80pnhwh95f743bg-spaghetti-extractor-dxball-semantic-qualified-runtime-provider-v2`,
proving byte-identical plans, sources, objects, receipts, and inventories. The
complete DX target and native PE32/Wine gates pass. Their combined 388.81
second rebuild was subsequently investigated with a reversible comment-only
edit confined to `candidate/runtime.py` plus the normal generated module-index
refresh. That controlled edit changed the qualified runtime provider and
downstream aggregate derivations, while the exact DX transfer-plan,
semantic-object, Behavioral-C, and linked-semantic-module derivation identities
all remained unchanged. Reverting the probe restored the original runtime and
aggregate identities. The earlier rebuild therefore reflected the same cut's
changes to shared external-contract code, which genuinely is upstream, rather
than an over-broad Python closure. No new closure/cache mechanism is required.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): the DirectDraw-tail blocker is now decomposed at the
checked boundary instead of being reported as a generic interface-binding
task. The exact local realization still needs the captured `ShowWindow` code
capability and frame; window and interface object selectors; the
`DirectDrawCreate` HRESULT/out-resource transducer; receiver class and
generation checks for interface methods; stack-local descriptor and output-cell
transducers; the `GetCaps` bit and global capability effects; six exact
zero-failure/one-success outcomes; and the two internal-call effects. These are
generic boundary/runtime capabilities, not permission to hide them inside the
authored C or to adopt the work-package's syntactic slot suggestions as
authority. The binding and its V5 index content-bind this causal inventory.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): the first checked service-transducer slice reuses the
existing service-event proof and native outgoing-call paths rather than adding
an interface-specific refinement system. The resolved external environment now
owns the one content-derived interface-method index consumed by the component
checker and renderer. A component binding selects an exact indirect-call event
and method digest; checking requires the exact vtable slot expression, fixed
PE32 ABI, logical/physical arity, live resource receiver, receiver class and
generation contract, and scalar EAX result. The materialized semantic service
is an ordinary checked external-call event carrying a typed interface identity,
so the existing path/refinement engine checks it without a parallel IR.

The shared runtime ABI now resolves and realizes an interface resource by
profile, interface class, object identity, and generation before the generic
thunk reads its vtable slot and enters the existing indirect-call dispatcher.
The synthetic proof/renderer tests, component and external-environment gates,
native runtime and PE32 DLL/Wine module gate, production lint, source-closure
gate, metadata gate, and complete GNU Hello and DX-Ball target gates pass. The
complete jq two-module migration gate also passes with its existing honest
blockers unchanged. The Hello real-scale replay passes in isolation
at 4.545 seconds and 510,200 KiB RSS; a 9.34-second concurrent attempt remains
classified as build contention under the existing eight-second isolated gate.
The Hello target's stale assertion for the retired V2 callable-domain spelling
was corrected to pin the compact V3 domain's 7,849 guest entries and 76 loader
targets. At that checkpoint, factory HRESULT/out-interface writeback,
stack-local descriptor/result cells, and resource-valued service results
remained deliberately unclaimed.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): the factory and first COM method slices now close
through that one shared transducer. `DirectDrawCreate` uses a private checked
out-interface cell, resolves the returned pointer only when the native
HRESULT succeeds, publishes the logical resource transactionally, restores
the caller frame, and rejects stale generations. The exact
`IDirectDraw::SetCooperativeLevel` event at RVA `0xcdba` is bound to slot 20,
offset 80, with its checked stdcall frame and live receiver. The DX work
package consequently has two mapped and four unmapped authored services; the
complete target gate passes without reducing either causal blocker.

The same transducer now has a conservative generic local-cell form. A cell is
accepted only at a profile-declared caller-memory argument, has 1..256 fully
initialized words, is allocated below the checked outgoing frame, and is
saved, initialized, observed, and restored transactionally. A typed
`local_cell_word` result may select only a word in that same writable cell.
The semantic contract turns every initial word into an exact machine guard
and binds the logical service result to the post-call memory projection.
Canonical calls that omit a redundant total argument-node list now use the
fixed checked ABI frame while retaining every sparse transfer stack-write
agreement. Focused parser, proof, failure, and C-renderer tests pass, and the
external renderer was split into its owning module rather than extending the
overlay monolith; both production modules are below the architecture ceiling.

This intentionally does not authorize DX-Ball's remaining descriptor calls.
The original `GetCaps` cell initializes only `DDCAPS.dwSize`; the rest is an
output region, and both `CreateSurface` calls initialize only fields selected
by `DDSURFACEDESC.dwFlags`. The pinned header AST proves pointer shape,
lifetime, and enclosing-object access, but not the read-before-write footprint
needed to replace those partial cells. Filling unspecified words with zero or
pretending the whole cell was initialized would strengthen the environment
and manufacture authority. The blocker is therefore narrowed to a reusable
profile-checked partial input/output cell contract. The authored draft has
also been corrected to use cooperative-level flag 8 and the machine
procedure's zero-on-failure/one-on-success convention; those source edits do
not reduce authority.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): the reviewed interface profile can now declare a
content-bound local-cell relation with an exact extent, read-before-call word
set, write-after-call word set, and either unconditional or successful-HRESULT
output validity. Extraction rejects relations that are unused, duplicated,
malformed, or inconsistent with the pinned AST pointer role, extent, access,
or lifetime. The binding, semantic checker, and C renderer consume that same
relation digest. Partial cells guard only declared inputs, never infer absent
bytes, and reject a result word whose failure value is not total. A compiled
native host vertical proves transactional save, initialization, provider
write, result observation, and restoration; the existing faithful and hybrid
PE32 DLL/Wine native-module gate and the complete DX-Ball target gate pass.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): the native PE32 vertical now exercises that partial
cell contract through the real loader and outgoing assembly bridge, not only
through the host mock. `IFixture::FillRecord` receives an eight-byte caller
cell whose first word is the sole declared input and whose second word is the
sole unconditional output. A dedicated completing export returns the native
mutation in both faithful generated C and the qualified portable-C overlay;
the pre-existing use-after-release export remains a separate fail-closed root
so its intentional rejection cannot masquerade as a writeback result. Both
faithful and hybrid candidates pass Wine, deployment, load observation, and
project completion. Component operations may now bind a strict declared
subset of the interface service catalog; disallowed service slots are null in
that operation's context, while every allowed service still requires one
exact machine binding. This removes irrelevant per-operation bindings without
weakening total provider selection.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): those two stronger facts now use one generic extension
of the same local-cell relation. A reviewed profile may declare disjoint
footprint variants selected by exact masked input-word discriminants, and it
may distinguish success-only outputs, failure-preserved initial values, and
failure-observed raw values. Initial words can come from exact checked entry
projections rather than invented constants. Parsing rejects overlapping or
unselected variants, undeclared reads or outputs, and any failure result whose
source was not initialized or explicitly admitted by the profile.

The two `CreateSurface` sites select the `dwFlags` variants `1` and `7` and
guard only the three- and five-word footprints actually consumed. `GetCaps`
uses the exact 95-word `DDCAPS` extent, initializes `dwSize` to 380, preserves
the entry value corresponding to `dwCaps`, and returns that word under the
profile's explicit failure-observed rule. Its result drives the original bit-25
projection and the three exact global state slots at RVAs 96764, 96772, and
96776. The parser, semantic contract, generated C, host-native transducer
fixture, PE32 DLL/Wine fixture, and complete Hello and DX-Ball gates pass.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): one logical service can now bind a canonical nonempty
inventory of exact machine events. This is part of the existing binding intent,
not another semantic or dispatch artifact. Every event must be unique and
ordered; the semantic contract checks every selected call, identity, frame,
transducer, and ABI, while the native thunk uses one equivalent checked runtime
representative. Exact event identities remain in the work package and provider
receipts. Legacy singleton `unit_id`/`event_index` fields are rejected, and all
Hello, jq, DX-Ball, test, and negative-fixture consumers were migrated in the
same clean cut.

That inventory binds all five path-specific `DestroyWindow` sites to one
logical service without duplicating the service contract.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): a generic finite-word transducer now relates a small
logical enum bijectively to exact physical machine words. Its parser requires
a canonical nonempty one-to-one map; the semantic contract inversely decodes
the machine word and guards membership in the admitted domain; generated C
switches the logical value back to the physical word and rejects every other
value. The existing compiled native transducer fixture exercises the mapping,
so it is not a DX-only presentation rule.

The authored `directdraw-init` source consequently names four typed failure
messages rather than passing HRESULT/status values as text pointers. Its
`MessageBoxA` provider binds the four corresponding call sites, their exact
image string anchors, the fixed caption anchor, and style zero. The later
merged `MessageBoxA` site remains outside this authored tail because it reports
the still-unimplemented `CreateClipper`, `SetHWnd`, and `SetClipper` failures.
All eight currently declared services are now mapped, while the work package
remains non-authorizing with the object, tail-service, internal-effect, and
outcome blockers intact. The complete DX-Ball gate passes.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): an existing external-call service may now name an
entry register or stack projection as its captured physical target. The
semantic contract requires every selected machine event to be an indirect call
to exactly that projection while retaining the resolved import identity and
physical ABI. Generated component C dispatches through the captured word, and
the shared runtime accepts it only when the same loader catalog row resolves
the declared DLL and symbol or ordinal. This is an identity-narrowed use of the
existing outgoing-call path, not a callback system, capability broker, or new
semantic language.

Authoritative disassembly exposes seven post-cut `ShowWindow` calls through the
entry `ESI` value: four precede the currently authored failure messages and
three belong to the unimplemented clipper tail. The earlier five-site count
was an undercount. One `hide_window(window)` service now binds the exact seven
event identities with command zero; the authored source invokes its first four
paths. Parser, semantic-contract, generated-thunk, runtime-source, DX-Ball,
native-module faithful/hybrid Wine, Hello, and jq gates pass.

A controlled build of this shared contract change advertised a 124-derivation
floating content-addressed graph but actually executed only eleven DX
presentation/component/work-package/regression builders. No ISA qualification,
original extraction, transfer-plan, semantic-object, linked-module,
Behavioral-C, or runtime builder ran. This confirms that advertised graph size
is not an invalidation count and that the existing closure boundary should be
retained.

Checkpoint (2026-09-02): all eleven DirectDraw-tail services now have exact
typed bindings. In addition to the earlier factory, cooperative-level,
capability, and surface calls, the binding covers `CreateClipper`, `SetHWnd`,
`SetClipper`, all seven captured-`ESI` `ShowWindow` sites, all five
`MessageBoxA` failure sites, and all five `DestroyWindow` sites. The structural
semantic contract is satisfied over the exact 65-transfer suffix. The finite
path checker now traverses every checked service and reaches the first of the
two target-owned success-tail calls at RVA `0xcfe7`; it no longer stops on an
untyped service, an RVA/VA alias mismatch, a partial local cell, an unchecked
call frame, or an instruction-local undefined flag.

That is still not portable-provider authority. The two success-tail calls
clear the target-owned object table and publish the primary surface in a
target-owned global before the original epilogue returns one. The authored C
does not perform either effect. A faithful generated-C continuation is the
preferred ownership split, but this component begins inside an existing
physical stack frame. Re-entering generated C therefore requires a checked
continuation portal that proves every live register, flag, stack, object, and
lifecycle fact expected by the target unit. A jump target by itself is not
such a proof. The target-owned blocker now names
`checked_faithful_continuation_portal_state` separately from the six outcome
relations and two internal effects so neither can be discharged by an
unchecked fallthrough. The complete DX-Ball gate remains the authority
boundary and continues to select generated Behavioral C.

Exit: DX-Ball uses the common semantic-module and deployment path and reports
only stable, causally distinct blockers for genuinely missing authority.

Status: complete on 2026-09-02. The complete DX-Ball gate passes with generated
Behavioral C explicitly selected and the portable DirectDraw overlay honestly
blocked by `checked_faithful_continuation_portal_state` plus its separately
reported outcome/internal-effect relations. No unchecked fallthrough or silent
provider fallback was introduced. Work item E is active.

### E. Retire and close the repository

Status: complete on 2026-09-02. The generated registry and metadata freshness
checks pass; the final non-authorizing target comparison is recorded in
[`baselines/2026-09-02-target-status-v3.md`](baselines/2026-09-02-target-status-v3.md).
The exact root flake check passes all evaluated checks and the exact target
flake check passes all 392 checks, including PE32/Wine integration, deployment and
candidate observation, performance, architecture, and retired-format absence.
`git diff --check` and the focused post-split test shards pass on the final
tree. No completion claim relies on the unavailable remote builder; Nix fell
back to the local builder and completed successfully.

- Migrate all remaining production consumers, then remove superseded V1 and
  V4/V5 readers, constructors, commands, formats, and compatibility paths.
- Retain the host evaluator and optional must analysis only in developer and
  test closures.
- Refresh the domain-owned format registry, Python module index, test
  manifest, repository map, target status snapshots, performance evidence,
  and documentation.
- Split unrelated oversized test modules without changing behavior so the
  affected and full suites can run.
- Run all final root and target Nix checks, PE32 ingress/project integration,
  applicable Wine candidate suites, metadata freshness, architecture and
  retired-format absence gates, deployment decoding, and candidate-observed
  project completion.

Exit: every completion requirement in the detailed plan is satisfied. Only at
that point may the active goal be marked complete.

## Completion state

No work remains inside this goal. Treat the floating derivation advertisement
as a plan, not an invalidation measurement; the controlled jq probe above is
the measured gate.

Do not implement a continuation portal merely to lower DX-Ball's blocker
count. It becomes in-scope only for a future request to authorize the portable
`directdraw-init` overlay, and then it must prove the complete physical state
relation against an exact transfer-plan target. For this goal, retain explicit
generated-C ownership, the honest blocker, total provider selection, and no
silent fallback.
